### I / 第一章

## In Which Things Happen Near Barking Creek / 巴金溪畔诸事纪

===Original===
The wind howled dismally round a house standing by itself almost on the shores of Barking Creek. It was the grey dusk of an early autumn day, and the occasional harsh cry of a seagull rising discordantly above the wind alone broke the silence of the flat, desolate waste.

The house seemed deserted. Every window was shuttered; the garden was uncared for and a mass of weeds; the gate leading on to the road, apparently feeling the need of a deficient top hinge, propped itself drunkenly on what had once been a flowerbed. A few gloomy trees swaying dismally in the wind surrounded the house and completed the picture﻿—one that would have caused even the least imaginative of men to draw his coat a little tighter round him, and feel thankful that it was not his fate to live in such a place.

But then few people ever came near enough to the house to realize its sinister appearance. The road﻿—it was little better than a cart track﻿—which passed the gate, was out of the beaten way; only an occasional fisherman or farm labourer ever used it, and that generally by day when things assumed their proper proportion, and it was merely an empty house gradually falling to pieces through lack of attention. At night they avoided it if possible; folks did say that twelve years ago some prying explorer had found the bones of a skeleton lying on the floor in one of the upstairs rooms with a mildewed rope fixed to one of the beams in the ceiling. And then it had been empty for twenty years.

Even now when the wind lay in the east or northeast and the tide was setting in, there were those who said that you could see a light shining through the cracks in the shutters in that room upstairs, and that, should a man climb up and look in, he’d see no skeleton, but a body with purple face and staring eyes swinging gently to and fro, and tied by the neck to a beam with a rope which showed no trace of mildew. Ridiculous, of course; but then so many of these local superstitions are. Useful, too, in some cases; they afford a privacy from the prying attention of local gossips far more cheaply and effectively than high walls and bolts and bars.

===Chinese===
风惨厉地呼啸着，绕着那幢孤零零、几乎就立在巴金溪（Barking Creek）岸边的房子打转。那是一个初秋日子的灰色黄昏，偶尔一声海鸥粗厉的啼鸣刺耳地盖过风声，划破这片平坦荒原上的死寂。

这房子像是已被弃置。每一扇窗都钉上了护窗板；园子无人照管，长满了荒草；通向道路的那扇大门，似乎是嫌自己的上铰链不大够用，醉汉似地倚靠在从前曾是一个花坛的地方。几棵阴沉的树在风中凄凉地摇曳，环绕着这所房子，凑成了一幅完整的画面——这画面足以叫最没有想象力的人也把大衣裹得更紧些，并暗自庆幸自己命不该住在这种地方。

不过话说回来，难得有人会走得离房子这么近，足以领教它那阴森可怖的模样。经过大门的那条路——说它是条路，其实和一条车辙道差不了多少——本就偏僻；只有偶尔路过的渔夫或农工才会走它，而且多半还是在白天，那时万物各归其位，这房子不过是一幢因年久失修而渐渐倾颓的空屋罢了。到了夜里，人们能避开就避开；大伙儿确实说，十二年前有某个爱管闲事的探秘者在楼上一个房间的地板上发现了一副骷髅的尸骨，天花板的横梁上还系着一根生了霉的绳子。自那以后，这房子又空了二十年。

即便到了如今，只要风从东面或东北面吹来、潮水上涨的时候，仍有人说，你能看见一道光从楼上那个房间护窗板的缝隙里透出来；还说，倘若有人爬上去往里张望，他看到的不会是骷髅，而是一具面孔发紫、双目圆睁的尸体，正被一根毫无霉迹的绳子吊住脖子，在横梁下轻轻地来回晃荡。这当然是荒唐话；可这一类的本地迷信大多如此。在某些情况下还颇有用处；它们比高墙、门栓和铁条都更廉价、更有效地，把爱饶舌的好事之客挡在了门外。

===Original===
So, at any rate, one of the two men who were walking briskly along the rough track seemed to think.

“Admirable,” he remarked, as he paused for a moment at the entrance of the weed-grown drive. “Quite admirable, my friend. A house situated as this one is, is an acquisition, and when it is haunted in addition it becomes a godsend.”

He spoke English perfectly with a slight foreign accent, and his companion nodded abruptly.

“From what I heard about it, I thought it would do,” he answered. “Personally I think it’s a damnable spot, but since you were so set against coming to London, I had to find somewhere in this neighbourhood.”

===Chinese===
至少，那两个正沿着崎岖小路快步走来的汉子当中，有一个是这么想的。

「妙极了，」他在那条杂草丛生的车道口停了片刻，说道，「真是妙极了，我的朋友。一幢位置如此这般的房子，可算是难得的收获；何况还闹鬼，那简直是天赐之物。」

他英语说得极好，只略带一点外国口音，他的同伴生硬地点了点头。

「听人说起这地方的时候，我就觉得它派得上用场，」他答道，「就我个人而言，我觉得这是个人见鬼的鬼地方；可你既然那么执意不肯上伦敦来，我只好在这附近找个地方。」

===Original===
The two men started to walk slowly up the drive. Branches dripping with moisture brushed across their faces, and involuntarily they both turned up the collars of their coats.

“I will explain my reasons in due course,” said the first speaker shortly. “You may take it from me that they were good. What’s that?”

He swung round with a little gasp, clutching his companion’s arm.

“Nothing,” cried the other irritably. For a moment or two they stood still, peering into the dark undergrowth. “What did you think it was?”

“I thought I heard a bush creaking as if﻿—as if someone was moving,” he said, relaxing his grip. “It must have been the wind, I suppose.”

He still peered fearfully into the gloomy garden, until the other man dragged him roughly towards the house.

“Of course it was the wind,” he muttered angrily. “For heaven’s sake, Zaboleff, don’t get the jumps. If you will insist on coming to an infernal place like this to transact a little perfectly normal business you must expect a few strange noises and sounds. Let’s get indoors; the others should be here by now. It oughtn’t to take more than an hour, and you can be on board again long before dawn.”

===Chinese===
两人开始慢慢沿着车道往上走。湿漉漉的枝条拂过他们的脸，两人都不由自主地把衣领竖了起来。

「我的理由到时候自会向你说明，」先开口的那位简短地说，「你只管相信我，那些理由都很充分。那是什么？」

他倒抽一口凉气，猛地转过身，一把抓住同伴的胳膊。

「没什么，」对方不耐烦地叫道。两人一动不动地站了片刻，朝黑暗的灌木丛里张望。「你以为是什么？」

「我以为我听见一丛灌木咯吱响了一声，好像——好像有人在动，」他松开手说道，「我想，准是风吧。」

他仍旧惊恐地朝那阴沉沉的园子里张望，直到对方粗鲁地把他拽向屋子。

「当然准是风了，」他气冲冲地嘟囔道，「看在上天的份上，扎博列夫（Zaboleff），别疑神疑鬼的。你既然非要上这么个鬼地方来办一桩再正常不过的小事，就得预备着听到些古里古怪的声响。咱们进屋吧；其他人该到了。这事儿用不了一个钟头，天亮之前你早就可以回到船上了。」

===Original===
The man who had been addressed as Zaboleff ceased looking over his shoulder, and followed the other through a broken-down lattice-gate to the rear of the house. They paused in front of the back door, and on it the leader knocked three times in a peculiar way. It was obviously a prearranged signal, for almost at once stealthy steps could be heard coming along the passage inside. The door was cautiously pulled back a few inches, and a man peered out, only to throw it open wide with a faint sigh of relief.

“It’s you, Mr. Waldock, is it?” he muttered. “Glad you’ve got ’ere at last. This place is fair giving us all the ’ump.”

“Evening, Jim.” He stepped inside, followed by Zaboleff, and the door closed behind them. “Our friend’s boat was a little late. Is everyone here?”

“Yep,” answered the other. “All the six of us. And I reckons we’d like to get it over as soon as possible. Has he”﻿—his voice sank to a hoarse undertone﻿—“has he brought the money?”

“You’ll all hear in good time,” said Waldock curtly. “Which is the room?”

“ ’Ere it is, guv’nor.” Jim flung open a door. “And you’ll ’ave to sit on the floor, as the chairs ain’t safe.”

===Chinese===
那个被唤作扎博列夫的人不再回头张望，跟着同伴穿过一扇破败的花格门，绕到屋后。他们在后门前停下，领头的人在门上以一种独特的方式敲了三下。这显然是事先约定的暗号，因为门内几乎立刻就传来一阵蹑手蹑脚的脚步声，沿着过道走近。门被小心翼翼地拉开几寸，一个人探出头来张望，随即如释重负地轻轻叹了口气，把门大敞开来。

「是您哪，沃尔多克（Waldok）先生，是吗？」他压低声音道，「您总算到了，可太好啦。这鬼地方真把我们一个个憋得直冒火。」

「晚上好，吉姆（Jim）。」他跨进门去，扎博列夫跟在身后，门在他们背后合上。「我们那位朋友的船晚了一点。人都齐了吗？」

「齐啦，」对方答道，「我们六个全到。我琢磨着咱们还是越快了结越好。他是不是」——他的嗓音压成了沙哑的低语——「他是不是把钱带来了？」

「到时候你们自会知道，」沃尔多克冷冷地说，「房间在哪儿？」

「这边，老爷。」吉姆一把推开门。「您几位只好坐地板，椅子靠不住。」

===Original===
Two candles guttered on a square table in the centre of the room, showing up the faces of the five men who sat on the floor, leaning against the walls. Three of them were nondescript specimens of humanity of the type that may be seen by the thousand hurrying into the City by the early business trains. They were representative of the poorer type of clerk﻿—the type which Woodbines its fingers to a brilliant orange; the type that screams insults at a football referee on Saturday afternoon. And yet to the close observer something more might be read on their faces: a greedy, hungry look, a shifty, untrustworthy look﻿—the look of those who are jealous of everyone better placed than themselves, but who are incapable of trying to better their own position except by the relative method of dragging back their more fortunate acquaintances; the look of little men dissatisfied not so much with their own littleness as with the bigness of other people. A nasty-faced trio with that smattering of education which is the truly dangerous thing; and﻿—three of Mr. Waldock’s clerks.

The two others were Jews; a little flashily dressed, distinctly addicted to cheap jewellery. They were sitting apart from the other three, talking in low tones, but as the door opened, their conversation ceased abruptly and they looked up at the newcomers with the keen, searching look of their race. Waldok they hardly glanced at; it was the stranger Zaboleff who riveted their attention. They took in every detail of the shrewd, foreign face﻿—the olive skin, the dark, piercing eyes, the fine-pointed beard; they measured him up as a boxer measures up his opponent, or a business man takes stock of the second party in a deal; then once again they conversed together in low tones which were barely above a whisper.

===Chinese===
屋子中央一张方桌上，两支蜡烛烛泪横流，照见五个靠着墙坐在地板上的人的脸。其中三个属于那种面目模糊的人物，乘早班业务火车涌进伦敦城的人群里，这种货色成千上万。他们是那种穷酸职员的典型——那种用「木莲」牌廉价烟把手指熏成鲜亮橙黄色的典型；那种星期六下午对着足球裁判破口大骂的典型。然而细心的观察者还能从他们脸上读出些别的东西：一种贪婪饥饿的神情，一种鬼祟不可信赖的神情——这种人嫉妒每一个境遇比自己好的人，却无力靠自己的努力改善处境，只会用「把比自己走运的熟人拖下水」这种相对的法子；这是小人物的眼神，他们与其说是对自己的渺小不满，不如说是对别人的高大不满。这三张脸生得一副歹相，又都带着那么一星半点的教育——这才是真正危险的东西；而且——他们是沃尔多克先生手下的三个职员。

另外两个是犹太人；衣着略微花哨，明显嗜好廉价首饰。他们与那三个人分开坐着，低声交谈，可门一开，话头便戛然而止，他们抬起头，用本民族那种犀利审视的目光打量着新来的两个人。沃尔多克他们几乎没看一眼；吸住他们注意的是那个陌生的扎博列夫。他们一一打量那张精明的异邦人脸——橄榄色的皮肤、漆黑犀利的眼睛、尖端细密的胡须；他们估量他，就像拳手估量对手，或生意人估量一笔买卖的另一方；然后他们又用低得几乎听不见的声音交谈起来。

===Original===
It was Jim who broke the silence﻿—Flash Jim, to give him the full name to which he answered in the haunts he frequented.

“Wot abaht getting on with it, guv’nor?” he remarked with an attempt at a genial smile. “This ’ere ’ouse ain’t wot I’d choose for a blooming ’oneymoon.”

With an abrupt gesture Waldock silenced him and advanced to the table.

“This is Mr. Zaboleff, gentlemen,” he said quietly. “We are a little late, I am afraid, but it was unavoidable. He will explain to you now the reason why you were asked to come here, and not meet at our usual rendezvous in Soho.”

He stepped back a couple of paces and Zaboleff took his place. For a moment or two he glanced round at the faces turned expectantly towards him; then resting his two hands on the table in front of him, he leaned forward towards them.

“Gentlemen,” he began, and the foreign accent seemed a little more pronounced, “I have asked you to come here tonight through my good friend, Mr. Waldock, because it has come to our ears﻿—no matter how﻿—that London is no longer a safe meeting-place. Two or three things have occurred lately the significance of which it is impossible to disregard.”

===Chinese===
打破沉默的是吉姆——说全了，是「闪电」吉姆（Flash Jim），在他常出没的窝窟里，他应的就是这个全名。

「咱们是不是该办正事啦，老爷？」他挤出一副讨好的笑容说道，「这破屋子可不是我会挑来度啥要命蜜月的地儿。」

沃尔多克一个生硬的手势止住了他，走到桌前。

「各位，这位是扎博列夫先生，」他平静地说，「恐怕我们迟到了一会儿，但实在无可避免。他现在就向各位说明，为什么要请诸位上这儿来，而不是照旧在我们苏荷区（Soho）的老地方碰头。」

他退后两步，扎博列夫站到他的位置上。有那么一会儿他扫视着一张张满怀期待转向他的脸；然后把双手撑在身前的桌上，朝众人俯下身去。

「各位，」他开了口，那点外国口音似乎更明显了些，「今晚我通过我的好朋友沃尔多克先生请诸位到此，是因为已有消息传到我们耳中——怎么传来的不必追究——伦敦已不再是个安全的会面之地。最近发生了两三件事，其意义不容忽视。」

===Original===
“Wot sort of things?” interrupted Flash Jim harshly.

“I was about to tell you,” remarked the speaker suavely, and Flash Jim subsided, abashed. “Our chief, with whom I spent last evening, is seriously concerned about these things.”

“You spent last night with the chief?” said Waldock, and his voice held a tremour of excitement, while the others leaned forward eagerly. “Is he, then, in Holland?”

“He was at six o’clock yesterday evening,” answered Zaboleff with a faint smile. “Today﻿—now﻿—I know no more than you where he is.”

“Who is he﻿—this man we’re always hearing about and never seeing?” demanded one of the three clerks aggressively.

“He is﻿—the Chief,” replied the other, while his eyes seemed to bore into the speaker’s brain. “Just that﻿—and no more. And that is quite enough for you.” His glance travelled round the room, and his audience relaxed. “By the way, is not that a chink in the shutter there?”

“All the safer,” grunted Flash Jim. “Anyone passing will think the ghost is walking.”

“Nevertheless, kindly cover it up,” ordered Zaboleff, and one of the Jews rose and wedged his pocket-handkerchief into the crack. There was silence in the room while he did so, a silence broken only by the mournful hooting of an owl outside.

“Owls is the only things wot comes to this damned museum,” said Flash Jim morosely. “Owls and blinking fools like us.”

“Stow it, Jim,” snarled Waldock furiously. “Anyone would think you wanted a nurse.”

===Chinese===
「啥样的事儿？」闪电吉姆粗声打断。

「我正要告诉各位，」说话的人温和地道，闪电吉姆讪讪地收了声。「我们的首领，我昨晚还和他在一起，他对这些事深为忧虑。」

「你昨晚和首领在一起？」沃尔多克说，嗓音里带着一丝激动的颤动，其余的人都急切地探身向前。「那么，他在荷兰（Holland）？」

「昨天傍晚六点的时候，他在，」扎博列夫带着一丝浅笑答道，「今天——眼下——他在哪儿，我和你们一样不知道。」

「他是谁——这个我们老听人提起、却从不照面的人？」三个职员中有一个挑衅地质问。

「他是——首领，」对方答道，眼睛似乎要钻进问话人的脑壳里去。「就是这个——没有别的。这对你来说已经足够。」他的目光扫过全屋，听众们松了口气。「顺便问一句，那边的护窗板上是不是有条缝？」

「那才更保险呢，」闪电吉姆咕哝道，「过路的人准以为鬼魂出来走动了。」

「尽管如此，还是把它遮上吧，」扎博列夫命令道，一个犹太人站起身，把自己的手帕塞进那条缝里。他塞手帕的时候，屋里一片寂静，只有外面猫头鹰一声声凄凉的啼叫打破了这沉寂。

「猫头鹰是这该死的博物馆里头唯一上门的东西，」闪电吉姆闷闷地说，「猫头鹰，还有咱们这种蠢透了的傻瓜。」

「闭嘴，吉姆，」沃尔多克怒喝道，「人家还以为你缺个奶妈呢。」

===Original===
“Gentlemen﻿—please.” Zaboleff held up a protesting hand. “We do not want to prolong matters, but one or two explanations are necessary. To return, then, to these things that have happened recently, and which necessitated a fresh rendezvous for this evening﻿—one which our friend Mr. Waldock so obligingly found. Three messengers sent over during the last three weeks bearing instructions and﻿—what is more important﻿—money, have disappeared.”

“Disappeared?” echoed Waldock stupidly.

“Absolutely and completely. Money and all. Two more have been abominably ill-treated and had their money taken from them, but for some reason they were allowed to go free themselves. It is from them that we have obtained our information.”

“Blimey!” muttered Flash Jim; “is it the police?”

“It is not the police, which is what makes it so much more serious,” answered Zaboleff quietly, and Flash Jim breathed a sigh of relief. “It is easy to keep within the law, but if our information is correct we are up against a body of men who are not within the law themselves. A body of men who are absolutely unscrupulous and utterly ruthless; a body of men who appear to know our secret plans as well as we do ourselves. And the difficulty of it is, gentlemen, that though, legally speaking, on account of the absurd legislation in this country we may keep within the law ourselves, we are hardly in a position to appeal to the police for protection. Our activities, though allowed officially, are hardly such as would appeal even to the English authorities. And on this occasion particularly that is the case. You may remember that the part I played in stirring up bloodshed at Cowdenheath a few months ago, under the name of MacTavish, caused me to be deported. So though our cause is legal﻿—my presence in this country is not. Which was why tonight it was particularly essential that we should not be disturbed. Not only are we all up against this unknown gang of men, but I, in addition, am up against the police.”

===Chinese===
「各位——请。」扎博列夫举起一只手示意众人安宁。「我们不想把事情拖长，但有一两件事必须说明。那么，回到刚才说的近来发生的那些事——正是这些事使我们今晚必须另觅一个新的碰头地点，也就是我们的朋友沃尔多克先生如此热心地为我们找到的这一处。过去三个星期里派过来的三个信使，带着指示，也带着——更要紧的——钱，全都失踪了。」

「失踪了？」沃尔多夫怔怔地应了一声。

「彻彻底底、干干净净地失踪了。钱也一并没了下落。还有两个人被恶毒地毒打了一顿，钱也被抢走了，但出于某种缘故，他们本人倒被放回了。我们正是从他们口中得到了消息。」

「我的天！」闪电吉姆嘟囔道，「莫不是警察？」

「不是警察，这正是事情格外棘手的地方，」扎博列夫平静地答道，闪电吉姆如释重负地松了口气。「守着法度办事本不算难，可要是我们的消息不假，那我们是在和一帮自己不守法度的人周旋。一帮全无顾忌、毫不留情的人；一帮仿佛和我们自己一样清楚我们秘密计划的人。棘手之处在于，各位，虽说从法律上讲，由于这个国家荒唐的立法，我们自己尚能守法，可我们几乎无法指望请警察来保护。我们的活动，虽说官方默许，却连英国当局也不会青眼相看。这一次尤其如此。各位大概记得，几个月前我在考登希思（Cowdenheath）煽动流血事件，用的是麦克塔维什（MacTavish）这个名字，结果被驱逐出境。所以，尽管我们的事业合法——我呆在这个国家却是不合法的。正因如此，今晚我们尤其不能被人打扰。我们这帮人不仅要对付那伙来路不明的匪帮，而且我本人还要应付警察。」

===Original===
“Have you any information with regard to this gang?” It was the Jew who had closed the chink in the shutters speaking for the first time.

“None of any use﻿—save that they are masked in black, and cloaked in long black cloaks.” He paused a moment as if to collect his thoughts. “They are all armed, and Petrovitch﻿—he was one of the men allowed to escape﻿—was very insistent on one point. It concerned the leader of the gang, whom he affirmed was a man of the most gigantic physical strength; a giant powerful as two ordinary strong men. He said,﻿ ﻿… *Ach! Mein Gott*﻿—!”

His voice rose to a scream as he cowered back, while the others, with terror on their faces, rose hurriedly from their seats on the floor and huddled together in the corners of the room.

In the doorway stood a huge man covered from head to foot in black. In each hand he held a revolver, with which he covered the eight occupants during the second or two which it took for half a dozen similarly disguised men to file past him, and take up their positions round the walls. And Waldok, a little more educated than the remainder of his friends, found himself thinking of old tales of the Spanish Inquisition and the Doges of Venice even as he huddled a little nearer to the table.

===Chinese===
「关于这帮人，您可有任何消息？」说话的是那个塞护窗板缝隙的犹太人，他这是第一次开口。

「有用的没有——只知道他们戴着黑色面罩，披着长长的黑斗篷。」他停了停，像是在理清思路。「他们都带着家伙，而彼得罗维奇（Petrovitch）——他是被放走的两个人之一——对一件事极是强调。那件事关涉到这伙人的头目，他断言那人膂力惊人、力大无穷；一个巨人，力气顶得上两个寻常壮汉。他说，……哎呀！我的天哪——！」

他这一声尖叫拔高了嗓门，整个人直缩向后，其余的人脸上尽是惊恐，急忙从地板上的座位上站起来，挤到屋角的角落里。

门口站着一个魁梧的大汉，从头到脚裹在黑衣之中。他两手各执一把左轮手枪，在这两把枪的笼罩之下看住了屋里这八个人——就那么一两秒钟工夫，六七个同样装束的人便从他身侧鱼贯而入，沿墙各就各位。沃尔多克比他那些同伴略多一些学识，发现自己即便在缩着身子往桌边靠的同时，竟还想起了西班牙宗教裁判所和威尼斯总督那些古老的故事。

===Original===
“Stand by the table, all of you.”

It was the man at the door who spoke in a curiously deep voice, and like sheep they obeyed him﻿—all save Flash Jim. For that worthy, crook though he was, was not without physical courage. The police he knew better than to play the fool with, but these were not the police.

“Wot the﻿—” he snarled, and got no further.

Something hit him behind the head, a thousand stars danced before his eyes, and with a strangled grunt he crashed forward on his face.

For a moment or two there was silence, and then once again the man at the door spoke.

“Arrange the specimens in a row.”

In a second the seven remaining men were marshalled in a line, while behind them stood six motionless black figures. And then the big man walked slowly down in front of them, peering into each man’s face. He spoke no word until he reached the end of the line, and then, his inspection concluded, he stepped back and leaned against the wall facing them.

===Chinese===
「都站到桌边来。」

是门口那人发话，嗓音低沉得异样。众人便如绵羊一般听从了——只有闪电吉姆例外。因为这位爷，虽是个混混，倒也不缺匹夫之勇。警察他懂得不能胡来，可眼下这几位不是警察。

「搞什么——」他龇牙咆哮，话没说完。

不知什么东西在他后脑勺上砸了一下，眼前金星乱舞，他闷哼一声，一头栽倒在地。

片刻的静默之后，门口那人又开了口。

「把标本排成一行。」

转眼之间，剩下的七个人就被排成了一列，他们身后立着六个纹丝不动的黑衣身影。然后那大汉在众人面前缓缓走过，端详着每一张脸。直到走到队列尽头，他一言未发；待检阅完毕，便退后一步，靠在他们对面的那堵墙上。

===Original===
“A nauseating collection,” he remarked thoughtfully. “A loathsome brood. What are the three undersized and shivering insects on the right?”

“Those are three of my clerks,” said Waldok with an assumption of angry bravado. “And I would like to know﻿—”

“In good time you will,” answered the deep voice. “Three of your clerks, are they; imbued with your rotten ideas, I suppose, and yearning to follow in father’s footsteps? Have we anything particular against them?”

There was no answer from the masked men, and the leader made a sign. Instantly the three terrified clerks were seized from behind and brought up to him, where they stood trembling and shaking in every limb.

“Listen to me, you three little worms.” With an effort they pulled themselves together; a ray of hope was dawning in their minds﻿—perhaps they were going to be let off easy. “My friends and I do not like you or your type. You meet in secret places and in your slimy minds you concoct foul schemes which, incredible though it may seem, have so far had more than a fair measure of success in this country. But your main idea is not the schemes, but the money you are paid to carry them out. This is your first and last warning. Another time you will be treated differently. Get out of here. And see you don’t stop.”

===Chinese===
「一屋子令人作呕的货色，」他若有所思地说，「一窝叫人恶心的东西。右边那三个矮小、直打哆嗦的虫子，是干什么的？」

「那是我手下的三个职员，」沃尔多克装出一副愤怒的硬气模样说道，「我倒想知道——」

「到时候你自会知道，」那低沉的嗓音答道，「你手下的三个职员，是吗；想必是灌满了你那一套烂思想，正巴巴地想步老爹的后尘吧？这几位，咱们有什么特别的账要算吗？」

蒙面人都没有作声，首领做了个手势。三个吓坏了的职员立刻被人从背后抓住，押到他面前，浑身哆嗦，四肢打颤。

「你们三个小蛆虫，听我说。」他们强打精神；一线希望在他们心里升起——也许这一回可以轻易饶过他们。「我和我的朋友们不喜欢你们这种人。你们在隐秘之处碰头，在你们那黏糊糊的脑瓜里炮制出肮脏的勾当，说来难以置信，这些勾当在这个国家至今竟得手得过了头。可你们心心念念的不是那些勾当，而是为干这些勾当拿到手的那笔钱。这是给你们的第一次警告，也是最后一次。下回可就要另作处置了。滚出去。记住，别停下来。」

===Original===
The door closed behind them and two of the masked men; there was the sound as of a boot being used with skill and strength, and cries of pain; then the door reopened and the masked men returned.

“They are gone,” announced one of them. “We helped them on their way.”

“Good,” said the leader. “Let us continue the inspection. What are these two Hebrews?”

A man from behind stepped forward and examined them slowly; then he came up to the leader and whispered in his ear.

“Is that so?” A new and terrible note had crept into the deep voice. “My friends and I do not like your trade, you swine. It is well that we have come provided with the necessary implement for such a case. Fetch the cat.”

In silence one of the men left the room, and as his full meaning came home to the two Jews they flung themselves grovelling on the floor, screaming for mercy.

“Gag them.”

The order came out sharp and clear, and in an instant the two writhing men were seized and gagged. Only their rolling eyes and trembling hands showed the terror they felt as they dragged themselves on their knees towards the impassive leader.

“The cat for cases of this sort is used legally,” he remarked. “We merely anticipate the law.”

With a fresh outburst of moans the two Jews watched the door open and the inexorable black figure come in, holding in his hand a short stick from which nine lashes hung down.

“Heavens!” gasped Waldok, starting forward. “What are you going to do?”

“Flog them to within an inch of their lives,” said the deep voice. “It is the punishment for their method of livelihood. Five and six﻿—take charge. After you have finished remove them in Number 3 car, and drop them in London.”

===Chinese===
门在他们和两个蒙面人身后关上；接着传来一阵皮靴被运用得既巧妙又用力的声响，还有几声惨叫；然后门又开了，蒙面人回来了。

「他们走啦，」其中一个通报，「我们送了他们一程。」

「好，」首领说，「咱们继续检阅。这两个希伯来人是干什么的？」

后面有一个人走上前，慢慢地审视他们；然后凑到首领耳边低语。

「是这样吗？」那低沉的嗓音里渗入了一种新的、可怖的声调。「我和我的朋友们不喜欢你们的营生，你们这两头猪。幸亏我们带着处置这种情况的必要家伙。去把九尾鞭拿来。」

一个人默默地走出了屋子。两个犹太人这才咂摸出这话的全部意味，扑通一声匍匐在地板上，凄厉地哀号求饶。

「把他们的嘴堵上。」

这道命令干脆利落，转眼间两个挣扎扭动的人就被按住、堵上了嘴。他们跪着朝那不动声色的首领爬过去，只剩那骨碌碌乱转的眼睛和颤抖的双手，还显出他们心里的恐惧。

「这一类的案子，依律本就是用九尾鞭伺候的，」他说，「我们不过是抢在法律前头罢了。」

两个犹太人又发出一阵哀号，眼看门开了，那个铁面无情的黑衣人走了进来，手里握着一根短棍，底下垂着九根皮条。

「老天！」沃尔多克倒抽一口气，跨步上前，「你们要干什么？」

「把他们抽到只剩半条命，」那低沉的嗓音说，「这是他们那营生应得的报应。五号、六号——把这事儿接过去。完事之后用三号车把他们弄走，丢到伦敦去。」

===Original===
Struggling impotently, the Jews were led away, and the leader passed on to the remaining two men.

“So, Zaboleff, you came after all. Unwise, surely, in view of the police?”

“Who are you?” muttered Zaboleff, his lips trembling.

“A specimen hunter,” said the other suavely. “I am making a collection of people like you. The police of our country are unduly kind to your breed, although they would not have been kind to you tonight, Zaboleff, unless I had intervened. But I couldn’t let them have you; you’re such a very choice specimen. I don’t think somehow that you’ve worked this little flying visit of yours very well. Of course I knew about it, but I must confess I was surprised when I found that the police did too.”

“What do you mean?” demanded the other hoarsely.

“I mean that when we arrived here we found to our surprise that the police had forestalled us. Popular house, this, tonight.”

“The police!” muttered Waldok dazedly.

“Even so﻿—led by no less a personage than Inspector McIver. They had completely surrounded the house, and necessitated a slight change in my plans.”

“Where are they now?” cried Waldok.

“Ah! Where indeed? Let us trust at any rate in comfort.”

===Chinese===
两个犹太人徒劳地挣扎着被押了下去，首领便转向剩下的两个人。

「这么说，扎博列夫，你到底还是来了。瞧警察的份上，这未免太不智了吧？」

「你是谁？」扎博列夫喃喃道，嘴唇直打哆嗦。

「一个标本猎手，」对方温和地答道，「我正在搜罗你们这一类人，凑成一整套藏品。我国的警察对你们这种货色未免太厚道了，不过今晚上他们对你可不会客气，扎博列夫——要不是我插了手的话。但我不能让他们把你弄走；你可是件难得的上品。我总觉得，你这趟短暂的小飞行办得不大漂亮。这事我当然知情；不过我得承认，当我发现警察也知情的时候，倒是吃了一惊。」

「你这是什么意思？」对方沙哑地质问。

「我的意思是，我们赶到这里的时候，惊讶地发现警察竟比我们先到了一步。这房子今晚上可真够热闹的。」

「警察！」沃尔多克茫然地喃喃道。

「不错——带队的不小，正是麦克艾弗督察（Inspector McIver）本人。他们把房子团团围住了，害得我不得不略改计划。」

「他们这会儿在哪儿？」沃尔多克叫道。

「哈！到底在哪儿呢？但愿他们至少舒舒服服地待着吧。」

===Original===
“By heaven!” said Zaboleff, taking a step forward. “As I asked you before﻿—who are you?”

“And as I told you before, Zaboleff, a collector of specimens. Some I keep; some I let go﻿—as you have already seen.”

“And what are you going to do with me?”

“Keep you. Up to date you are the cream of my collection.”

“Are you working with the police?” said the other dazedly.

“Until tonight we have not clashed. Even tonight, well, I think we are working towards the same end. And do you know what that end is, Zaboleff?” The deep voice grew a little sterner. “It is the utter, final overthrow of you and all that you stand for. To achieve that object we shall show no mercy. Even as you are working in the dark﻿—so are we. Already you are frightened; already we have proved that you fear the unknown more than you fear the police; already the first few tricks are ours. But you still hold the ace, Zaboleff﻿—or shall we say the King of Trumps? And when we catch him you will cease to be the cream of my collection. This leader of yours﻿—it was what Petrovitch told him, I suppose, that made him send you over.”

“I refuse to say,” said the other.

“You needn’t; it is obvious. And now that you are caught﻿—he will come himself. Perhaps not at once﻿—but he will come. And then﻿ ﻿… But we waste time. The money, Zaboleff.”

===Chinese===
「老天在上！」扎博列夫向前迈了一步，「我方才问过你——你究竟是谁？」

「我也方才告诉过你了，扎博列夫，一个搜罗标本的人。有的我留着；有的我放走——这一点你已经看到了。」

「那你打算把我怎么样？」

「留着。时至今日，你是我这套藏品里头顶尖的上品。」

「你是和警察一伙儿的？」对方茫然地问。

「直到今夜为止，我们还没撞过车。就算是今夜，呃，我想我们也是在朝同一个目标使劲。你可知道那目标是什么，扎博列夫？」那低沉的嗓音严峻了些。「就是把你们，以及你们所代表的一切，彻底地、最终地打翻在地。为了这个目标，我们决不手软。正如你们在暗地里活动——我们也在暗地里活动。你们已经慌了；我们已经证明了，你们对这未知的恐惧甚于对警察的恐惧；头几张牌我们已经赢到手了。可你们手里还攥着那张王牌呢，扎博列夫——或者我们该说，王牌之王？等我们逮到他，你就不再是我这套藏品里的上品了。你们那位首领——想必是彼得罗维奇告诉他的那些话，才让他把你派过来的吧。」

「我不说，」对方道。

「你不必说；这是明摆着的事。如今你已经被擒——他会亲自来的。也许不是马上——可他一定会来。到那时候……可我们耽误工夫了。钱呢，扎博列夫。」

===Original===
“I have no money,” he snarled.

“You lie, Zaboleff. You lie clumsily. You have quite a lot of money brought over for Waldok so that he might carry on the good work after you had sailed tomorrow. Quick, please; time passes.”

With a curse Zaboleff produced a small canvas bag and held it out. The other took it and glanced inside.

“I see,” he said gravely. “Pearls and precious stones. Belonging once, I suppose, to a murdered gentlewoman whose only crime was that she, through no action of her own, was born in a different sphere to you. And, you reptile”﻿—his voice rose a little﻿—“you would do that here.”

Zaboleff shrank back, and the other laughed contemptuously.

“Search him﻿—and Waldok too.”

Two men stepped forward quickly.

“Nothing more,” they said after a while. “Except this piece of paper.”

There was a sudden movement on Zaboleff’s part﻿—instantly suppressed, but not quite soon enough.

“Injudicious,” said the leader quietly. “Memory is better. An address, I see﻿—No. 5, Green Street, Hoxton. A salubrious neighbourhood, with which I am but indifferently acquainted. Ah! I see my violent friend has recovered.” He glanced at Flash Jim, who was sitting up dazedly, rubbing the back of his head. “Number 4, the usual.”

There was a slight struggle, and Flash Jim lay back peacefully unconscious, while a faint smell of chloroform filled the room.

===Chinese===
「我没钱，」他龇牙道。

「你撒谎，扎博列夫。你撒谎撒得笨透了。你带过来好一大笔钱是给沃尔多克的，好让他等你明儿开船之后接着干这桩好事。快，劳驾；时间不等人。」

扎博列夫咒骂了一声，掏出一个小帆布袋递了过去。对方接过来，朝袋里瞥了一眼。

「我明白了，」他正色道，「珍珠和宝石。我料想原本是属于一位被谋害的淑女的吧，她唯一的罪名，便是并非出于自身缘故，生在了和你不同的阶层。而且，你们这条爬虫——」他的嗓音高了些，「你们居然想在这儿也来这一套。」

扎博列夫直缩回去，对方鄙夷地笑了。

「搜身——沃尔多克也搜。」

两个人立刻走上前来。

「没有别的了，」过了一会儿他们说，「除了这张纸。」

扎博列夫猛地一动——随即按捺下去，可惜慢了半拍。

「欠考虑啊，」首领平静地说，「还是靠记性更稳妥。一个地址，我瞧——霍克斯顿（Hoxton）格林街（Green Street）五号。一个挺宜人的地段，可惜我不大熟。哈！我看我那位动武的朋友已经醒过来了。」他瞟了闪电吉姆一眼，后者正发着怔坐起来，揉着后脑勺。「四号，老规矩。」

略微挣扎了一下，闪电吉姆便又安安稳稳地昏了过去，屋里弥漫起一股淡淡的氯仿气味。

===Original===
“And now I think we will go. A most successful evening.”

“What are you going to do with me, you scoundrel?” spluttered Waldok. “I warn you that I have influential friends, who﻿—who will ask questions in﻿—in Parliament if you do anything to me; who will go to Scotland Yard.”

“I can assure you, Mr. Waldok, that I will make it my personal business to see that their natural curiosity is gratified,” answered the leader suavely. “But for the present I fear the three filthy rags you edit will have to be content with the office boy as their guiding light. And I venture to think they will not suffer.”

He made a sudden sign, and before they realized what was happening the two men were caught from behind and gagged. The next instant they were rushed through the door, followed by Flash Jim. For a moment or two the eyes of the leader wandered round the now empty room taking in every detail: then he stepped forward and blew out the two candles. The door closed gently behind him, and a couple of minutes later two cars stole quietly away from the broken-down gate along the cart track. It was just midnight; behind them the gloomy house stood up gaunt and forbidding against the darkness of the night sky. And it was not until the leading car turned carefully into the main road that anyone spoke.

“Deuced awkward, the police being there.”

The big man who was driving grunted thoughtfully.

“Perhaps,” he returned. “Perhaps not. Anyway, the more the merrier. Flash Jim all right?”

“Sleeping like a child,” answered the other, peering into the body of the car.

===Chinese===
「好了，我想我们该走了。一个收获颇丰的夜晚。」

「你要把我怎么样，你这恶棍？」沃尔多克气急败坏地说，「我警告你，我有些有头有脸的朋友，他们——他们会到——到议会（Parliament）里去质询，要是你对我怎么样的话；他们会上苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）。」

「沃尔多克先生，我向你担保，他们那份自然的好奇心能否得到满足，我一定会亲自过问，」首领温文地答道，「不过眼下，恐怕您主编的那三份肮脏小报，只好委屈由办公室的小厮来当指路明灯了。我敢冒昧地说一句，它们不会因此有什么损失。」

他突然打了个手势，还没等他们明白是怎么回事，两个人就被从背后抓住、堵上了嘴。下一刻他们就被挟出了门，闪电吉姆也跟着被抬了出去。首领的目光在如今空无一人的屋里扫了一圈，把每个细节都看在眼里；然后他上前吹灭了两支蜡烛。门在他身后轻轻合上，几分钟之后，两辆汽车悄然驶出那扇破败的大门，沿着车辙道而去。此时正是午夜；他们身后，那阴森的房子在夜空的黑暗映衬下，瘦骨嶙峋地矗立着，可憎可怖。直到前面那辆车小心地拐上大路，才有人开口。

「警察在那儿，真要命地碍事。」

开车的那位大汉若有所思地哼了一声。

「也许是，」他答道，「也许不是。不管怎样，人越多越热闹。闪电吉姆没事吧？」

「睡得像个小娃娃似的，」对方朝车厢里张望了一眼答道。

===Original===
For about ten miles they drove on in silence: then at a main crossroads the car pulled up and the big man got out. The second car was just behind, and for a few moments there was a whispered conversation between him and the other driver. He glanced at Zaboleff and Waldok, who appeared to be peacefully sleeping on the back seat, and smiled grimly.

“Good night, old man. Report as usual.”

“Right,” answered the driver. “So long.”

The second car swung right-handed and started northwards, while the leader stood watching the vanishing tail lamp. Then he returned to his own seat, and soon the first beginnings of outer London were reached. And it was as they reached Whitechapel that the leader spoke again with a note of suppressed excitement in his voice.

“We’re worrying ’em; we’re worrying ’em badly. Otherwise they’d never have sent Zaboleff. He was too big a man to risk, considering the police.”

“It’s the police that I *am* considering,” said his companion.

The big man laughed.

“Leave that to me, old man; leave that entirely to me.”

===Chinese===
他们又默默地开了大约十英里：然后在一条主干道十字路口，车停了下来，那大汉下了车。第二辆车紧随其后，他和另一个司机低声交谈了片刻。他朝后座上貌似正安稳熟睡的扎博列夫和沃尔多克瞥了一眼，冷冷地笑了。

「晚安，老伙计。照常汇报。」

「好，」司机答道，「回头见。」

第二辆车向右一拐，朝北驶去，首领站在原地目送那盏尾灯消失。然后他回到自己的车上，没多久，伦敦外围最初的几处房舍便出现在眼前。直到他们抵达白教堂区（Whitechapel），首领才又开了口，嗓音里带着一丝压抑的兴奋。

「我们把它们搅得不安生了；搅得它们够呛。要不然，他们绝不会派扎博列夫来。考虑到警察这一层，他是个太大的人物，本不该冒这个险。」

「我正在考虑的，正是警察这一层，」他的同伴说。

大汉笑了起来。

「这事儿交给我，老伙计；这一层完全交给我好了。」

### II / II

In Which Scotland Yard Sits Up and Takes Notice / 苏格兰场大吃一惊

===Original===
Sir Bryan Johnstone leaned back in his chair and stared at the ceiling with a frown. His hands were thrust deep into his trouser pockets; his long legs were stretched out to their full extent under the big roll-top desk in front of him. From the next room came the monotonous tapping of a typewriter, and after a while Sir Bryan closed his eyes.

Through the open window there came the murmur of the London traffic﻿—that soothing sound so conducive to sleep in those who have lunched well. But that did not apply to the man lying back in his chair. Sir Bryan’s lunch was always a frugal meal, and it was no desire for sleep that made the Director of Criminal Investigation close his eyes. He was puzzled, and the report lying on the desk in front of him was the reason.

For perhaps ten minutes he remained motionless, then he leaned forward and touched an electric bell. Instantly the typewriter ceased, and a girl secretary came quickly into the room.

===Chinese===
布莱恩·约翰斯通爵士（Sir Bryan Johnstone）靠在椅背上，皱着眉头盯着天花板。他的双手深深插在裤袋里；两条长腿在身前那张带卷帘的宽大写字桌下伸得笔直。隔壁房间传来打字机单调的嗒嗒声，过了一会儿，布莱恩爵士闭上了眼。

敞开的窗外飘进伦敦车马的嗡嗡低语——那是吃过一顿丰盛午餐的人最容易安眠的声响。可这并不适用于椅子上这位躺着休息的人。布莱恩爵士的午餐向来简朴，让这位刑事调查局局长合上双眼的，也绝非睡意。他困惑不已，而摆在他桌上的那份报告，正是缘由。

他一动不动地坐了约莫十分钟，然后探身按响了电铃。打字声立刻停了下来，一位女秘书快步走进房间。

===Original===
“Miss Forbes,” said Sir Bryan, “I wish you would find out if Chief Inspector McIver is in the building. If so I would like to see him at once; if not, see that he gets the message as soon as he comes in.”

The door closed behind the girl, and after a moment or two the man rose from his desk and began to pace up and down the room with long, even strides. Every now and then he would stop and stare at some print on the wall, but it was the blank stare of a man whose mind is engrossed in other matters.

And once while he stood looking out of the window, he voiced his thoughts, unconscious that he spoke aloud.

“Dash it, McIver’s not fanciful. He’s the least fanciful man we’ve got. And yet﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「福布斯小姐（Miss Forbes），」布莱恩爵士说，「麻烦你去看看总督察麦克艾弗（McIver）在不在楼里。在的话，我立刻见他；不在的话，等他一回来就把口信交给他。」

门在女孩身后关上，过了一会儿，男人从桌前起身，迈着修长而均匀的步子在房间里来回踱步。他不时停下脚步，盯着墙上的某幅版画，那却是心不在焉的呆视——他脑子里盘算的完全是别的事。

还有一回，他站在窗前向外望去时，竟把心里的话说出了声，自己却浑然不觉。

「真见鬼，麦克艾弗可不是爱瞎想的人。他是我手下最不爱瞎想的一个。可是……」

===Original===
His eyes came round to the desk once more, the desk on which the report was lying. It was Inspector McIver’s report﻿—hence his instructions to the secretary. It was the report on a very strange matter which had taken place the previous night, and after a while Sir Bryan picked up the typed sheets and glanced through them again. And he was still standing by the desk, idly turning over the pages when the secretary came into the room.

“Chief Inspector McIver is here, Sir Bryan,” she announced.

“Tell him to come in, Miss Forbes.”

===Chinese===
他的目光又转回到桌上——报告就摊在那里。那是麦克艾弗督察的报告——所以他才对秘书作了那样的吩咐。报告所写，是昨夜发生的一桩极为离奇的事。过了一阵，布莱恩爵士拿起那几页打字稿，又翻看了一遍。秘书再进屋时，他还站在桌旁，百无聊赖地翻着那几页纸。

「麦克艾弗总督察到了，布莱恩爵士。」她通报。

「让他进来吧，福布斯小姐。」

===Original===
Certainly the Inspector justified his Chief’s spoken thought﻿—a less fanciful looking man it would have been hard to imagine. A square-jawed, rugged Scotchman, he looked the type to whom Holy Writ was Holy Writ only in so far as it could be proved. He was short and thickset, and his physical strength was proverbial. But a pair of kindly twinkling eyes belied the gruff voice. In fact, the gruff voice was a pose specially put on which deceived no one; his children all imitated it to his huge content, though he endeavoured to look ferocious when they did so. In short, McIver, though shrewd and relentless when on duty, was the kindest-hearted of men. But he was most certainly not fanciful.

===Chinese===
这位督察倒当真印证了上司方才那句心里话——再想找一个看上去更不爱瞎想的人，恐怕难上加难。他是个下颌方正、相貌粗犷的苏格兰人，一副只认证据、不认什么圣典的样子。他个头不高、敦实壮硕，力气大是出了名的。可一双和善、爱眨巴的眼睛，却把那副粗嗓门的架子戳了个穿。其实那粗嗓门是故意端出来的，谁也唬不住；他的几个孩子都学他这副腔调，他乐在其中，却还要在孩子们学他时装出一副凶相。总之，麦克艾弗办案时精明狠辣，骨子里却是天字号的大好人。但他绝、不、爱、瞎、想。

===Original===
“What the dickens is all this about, McIver,” said Sir Bryan with a smile, when the door had shut behind the secretary.

“I wish I knew myself, sir,” returned the other seriously. “I’ve never been so completely defeated in my life.”

Sir Bryan waved him to a chair and sat down at the desk.

“I’ve read your report,” he said, still smiling, “and frankly, McIver, if it had been anyone but you, I should have been annoyed. But I know you far too well for that. Look here”﻿—he pushed a box of cigarettes across the table﻿—“take a cigarette and your time and let’s hear about it.”

===Chinese===
「这到底是怎么回事啊，麦克艾弗？」门在秘书身后合上后，布莱恩爵士笑着问道。

「我自己也想知道啊，先生。」对方一本正经地答道，「我这辈子从没栽得这么惨过。」

布莱恩爵士示意他坐下，自己在桌前落座。

「报告我看过了。」他说，仍在笑着，「老实讲，麦克艾弗，换了别人交这东西上来，我可要发火了。但你我太熟了，我犯不着。来——」他把一盒香烟推过桌面，「抽根烟，慢慢说，让我听听是怎么一档子事。」

===Original===
McIver lit a cigarette and seemed to be marshalling his thoughts. He was a man who liked to tell his story in his own way, and his chief waited patiently till he was ready. He knew that when his subordinate did start he would get a clear, concise account of what had taken place, with everything irrelevant ruthlessly cut out. And if there was one thing that roused Sir Bryan to thoughts of murder and violence, it was a rambling, incoherent statement from one of his men.

“Well, sir,” began McIver at length, “this is briefly what took place. At ten o’clock last night as we had arranged, we completely surrounded the suspected house on the outskirts of Barking. I had had a couple of good men on duty there lying concealed the whole day, and when I arrived at about nine-thirty with Sergeant Andrews and half-a-dozen others, they reported to me that at least eight men were inside, and that Zaboleff was one of them. He had been shadowed the whole way down from Limehouse with another man, and both the watchers were positive that he had not left the house. So I posted my men and crept forward to investigate myself. There was a little chink in the wooden shutters of one of the downstairs rooms through which the light was streaming. I took a glimpse through, and found that everything was just as had been reported to me. There were eight of them there, and an unpleasant-looking bunch they were, too. Zaboleff I saw at the head of the table, and standing next to him was that man Waldock who runs two or three of the worst of the Red papers. There was also Flash Jim, and I began to wish I’d brought a few more men.”

===Chinese===
麦克艾弗点上一根烟，像是在梳理思路。他这人讲故事非得按自己的路子来不可，上司便耐心等着他开口。他知道这位下属一旦开讲，准会把事情的前前后后交代得清楚扼要，不相干的枝节一概砍个干净。而要说什么事能把布莱恩爵士惹得动了杀心、想揍人，那就是手底下的人交来一份东拉西扯、语无伦次的报告。

「是这样的，先生。」麦克艾弗终于开了口，「事情的大概经过是这样。昨晚十点，按我们事先的部署，我们把巴金（Barking）郊外那栋可疑的房子团团围住了。白天我一直派了两个得力的人埋伏在那儿盯着，等九点半我带着安德鲁斯巡佐（Sergeant Andrews）和另外六七个人赶到，他们向我汇报，说屋里至少有八个人，扎博列夫（Zaboleff）也在其中。他从莱姆豪斯（Limehouse）一路被人陪着、和另一个人一同被跟踪过来，两个盯梢的都打包票说他没离开过这房子。于是我把人手布置好，自己摸上前去探个究竟。楼下有间屋子的木百叶窗裂开一条小缝，灯光正从那里漏出来。我凑过去一看，情形果然如汇报的那样。屋里八个人，一伙面目可憎的家伙。我看见扎博列夫坐在桌子首位，紧挨着他站着的，是那个沃尔多克（Waldock）——旗下有两三份最恶劣的赤色报纸的那位。闪电吉姆（Flash Jim）也在，我当时就开始后悔没多带几个人了。」

===Original===
McIver smiled ruefully. “It was about the last coherent wish I remember. And,” he went on seriously, “what I’m going to tell you now, sir, may seem extraordinary and what one would expect in detective fiction, but as sure as I am sitting in this chair, it is what actually took place. Somewhere from close to, there came the sound of an owl hooting. At the same moment I distinctly heard the noise of what seemed like a scuffle, and a stifled curse. And then﻿—and this is what beats me, sir,” McIver pounded a huge fist into an equally huge palm, “I was picked up from behind as if I was a baby. Yes, sir, a baby.”

Involuntarily Sir Bryan smiled.

“You make a good substantial infant, McIver.”

===Chinese===
麦克艾弗苦笑了一下。「这大概是我记得的最后一个还算清醒的愿望了。还有，」他正色接下去，「我接下来要跟您说的，先生，听着可能荒唐，像是侦探小说里才有的情节，可我坐在您面前这门椅子上，句句属实。就在附近什么地方，传来一声猫头鹰的叫声。同一时刻，我清清楚楚听到一阵像是扭打的动静，还有一声压低的咒骂。然后——这一段才是最让我想不通的，先生。」麦克艾弗把一只大拳头狠狠擂进另一只同样宽大的掌心，「我从背后被人提了起来，跟提个婴孩似的。没错，先生，一个婴孩。」

布莱恩爵士不由得笑了。

「你这婴孩可够敦实够沉的，麦克艾弗。」

===Original===
“Exactly, sir,” grunted the Inspector. “If a man had suggested such a thing to me yesterday I’d have laughed in his face. But the fact remains that I was picked up just like a child in arms, and doped, sir, doped. Me﻿—at my time of life. They chloroformed me, and that was the last I saw of Zaboleff or the rest of the gang.”

“Yes, but it’s the rest of the report that beats me,” said his chief thoughtfully.

“So it does me, sir,” agreed McIver. “When I came to myself early this morning I didn’t realize where I was. Of course my mind at once went back to the preceding night, and what with feeling sick as the result of the chloroform, and sicker at having been fooled, I wasn’t too pleased with myself. And then I rubbed my eyes and pinched myself, and for a moment or two I honestly thought I’d gone off my head. There was I sitting on my own front door step, with a cushion all nicely arranged for my head and every single man I’d taken down with me asleep on the pavement outside. I tell you, sir, I looked at those eight fellows all ranged in a row for about five minutes before my brain began to act. I was simply stupefied. And then I began to feel angry. To be knocked on the head by a crew like Flash Jim might happen to anybody. But to be treated like naughty children and sent home to bed was a bit too much. Dammit, I thought, while they were about it, why didn’t they tuck me up with my wife.”

===Chinese===
「一点不错，先生。」督察咕哝道，「昨天要是有人跟我说会有这种事，我非当面笑话他不可。可事实就是，我像抱个孩子似的被人提了起来，然后被下了药，先生，下了药。我——活了大半辈子了。他们给我用了氯仿，等我一睁眼，扎博列夫和那一伙人，我连影儿都没再见到。」

「是啊，可报告后半截那一段才叫我想不通呢。」上司若有所思地说。

「我也一样，先生。」麦克艾弗附和，「今儿一大早我醒过来的时候，压根不知道自己在哪儿。脑子里自然立刻回到了头天夜里，一边是氯仿过后的恶心，一边是被人家当猴耍的窝囊，那滋味别提多难受了。接着我揉了揉眼，掐了掐自己，有那么一两分钟，我当真以为自己疯了。我居然坐在自家的门阶上，脑袋底下还垫了个软垫，软垫摆得规规矩矩；我带去的每一个人，全在外头人行道上呼呼大睡。我跟您说，先生，我盯着那八个家伙一排排躺着看了足有五分钟，脑子才转过来。我都懵了。然后火气才上来。被闪电吉姆那伙人敲闷棍，这种事谁碰上都不奇怪。可被当成淘气孩子打发回家睡觉，这就太过分了。妈的，我心里想，他们既然这么周全，怎么不索性把我塞进被窝，跟我老婆挨一块儿呢。」

===Original===
Once again Sir Bryan smiled, but the other was too engrossed to notice.

“It was then I saw the note,” continued McIver. He fumbled in his pocket, and his chief stretched out his hand to see the original. He already knew the contents almost by heart, and the actual note itself threw no additional light on the matter. It was typewritten, and the paper was such as can be bought by the ream at any cheap stationer’s.

“To think of an old bird like you, Mac,” it ran, “going and showing yourself up in a chink of light. You must tell Mrs. Mac to get some more cushions. There were only enough in the parlour for you and Andrews. I have taken Zaboleff and Waldock, and I dropped Flash Jim in Piccadilly Circus. I flogged two of the others whose method of livelihood failed to appeal to me; the remaining small fry I turned loose. Cheer oh! old son. The fellow in St. James’s makes wonderful pick-me-ups for the morning after. Hope I didn’t hurt you.”

===Chinese===
布莱恩爵士又一次笑了，可对方太沉浸在自己的话里，没察觉。

「就在那时候，我看见了那张条子。」麦克艾弗接着说。他在口袋里摸索着，上司伸手要原件来看。内容他早几乎背得烂熟，条子本身也并没提供什么新线索。那是打字机打的，纸张是任何廉价文具店论令卖的那种。

条子上写着：「麦老兄，想不到你这种老江湖，竟然跑到一线光缝里去露脸。得跟麦太太说一声，让她多备几个软垫。客厅里那点垫子，只够你和安德鲁斯用。我把扎博列夫和沃尔多克带走了，闪电吉姆我丢在了皮卡迪利广场（Piccadilly Circus）。另外两个我看不顺眼他们那营生的，抽了一顿鞭子；剩下的小鱼小虾我就放了。开心点，老伙计！圣詹姆斯（St. James's）那家店做的醒酒汤第二天早上喝灵得很。希望没把你弄疼。」

===Original===
Idly Sir Bryan studied the note, holding it up to the light to see if there was any watermark on the paper which might help. Then he studied the typed words, and finally with a slight shrug of his shoulders he laid it on the desk in front of him.

“An ordinary Remington, I should think. And as there are several thousands in use it doesn’t help much. What about Flash Jim?”

McIver shook his head.

“The first thing I did, sir, was to run him to ground. And I put it across him good and strong. He admitted everything: admitted he was down there, but over the rest of the show he swore by everything that he knew no more than I did. All he could say was that suddenly the room seemed full of men. And the men were all masked. Then he got a clip over the back of the head, and he remembers nothing more till the policeman on duty at Piccadilly Circus woke him with his boot just before dawn this morning.”

===Chinese===
布莱恩爵士漫不经心地端详着那张条子，把它举到亮处，想看看纸上有没有什么能帮忙的水印。然后他又研究起打字的字迹，最后微微一耸肩，把它放回桌上。

「我看是台普通的雷明顿（Remington）打字机。这种在用的有好几千台，帮不上什么忙。闪电吉姆那边怎么样？」

麦克艾弗摇摇头。

「我头一件事就是把他揪出来了，先生。我好好收拾了他一顿。他什么都认了：承认自己昨晚就在那儿，可后头那一出，他对天发誓说自己跟我一样一无所知。他只说突然间屋里像是挤满了人，那些人都蒙着面。接着他后脑勺挨了一下，之后就什么都不记得了，直到今儿天快亮，皮卡迪利广场上当班的警察一脚把他踹醒。」

===Original===
“Which fact, of course, you have verified,” said Sir Bryan.

“At once, sir,” answered the other. “For once in his life Flash Jim appears to be speaking the truth. Which puts a funny complexion on matters, sir, if he is speaking the truth.”

The Inspector leaned forward and stared at his chief.

“You’ve heard the rumours, sir,” he went on after a moment, “the same as I have.”

“Perhaps,” said Sir Bryan quietly. “But go on, McIver. I’d like to hear what’s on your mind.”

“It’s the Black Gang, sir,” said the Inspector, leaning forward impressively.

===Chinese===
「这件事，你当然核实过了。」布莱恩爵士说。

「马上就核实的，先生。」对方答道，「闪电吉姆这回倒是头一回说了真话。要是他说的是真话，先生，这事可就透着古怪了。」

督察探过身去，盯着自己的上司。

「那些传闻您也听说了，先生。」他顿了顿又说，「跟我一样。」

「也许吧。」布莱恩爵士不动声色地说，「往下说，麦克艾弗。我想听听你心里怎么盘算的。」

「是黑帮（The Black Gang）干的，先生。」督察郑重其事地探身说道。

===Original===
“There have been rumours going round, rumours which our men have heard here and there for the past two months. I’ve heard ’em myself; and once or twice I’ve wondered. Now I’m sure﻿—especially after what Flash Jim said. That gang is no rumour, it’s solid fact.”

“Have you any information as to what their activities have been, assuming for a moment it is the truth?” asked Sir Bryan.

“None for certain, sir; until this moment I wasn’t certain of its existence. But now﻿—looking back﻿—there have been quite a number of sudden disappearances. We haven’t troubled officially, we haven’t been asked to. Hardly likely when one realizes who the people are who have disappeared.”

“All conjecture, McIver,” said Sir Bryan. “They may be lying doggo, or they’ll turn up elsewhere.”

===Chinese===
「这一阵一直有风声，先生，这两个月我们的人东一句西一句都听到过。我也亲耳听过；有那么一两次我还犯过嘀咕。现在我敢肯定了——尤其是听了闪电吉姆那番话之后。那伙人不是什么传闻，是确确实实的存在。」

「假设它是真的，你手里有没有什么线索，能说明他们都干过些什么？」布莱恩爵士问。

「确切的说不上来，先生；到这会儿为止，我连它存不存在都拿不准。可现在——回过头看——已经有过好些莫名其妙的人失踪了。我们没正式立案，也没人请我们立。想想那些失踪的是什么人，也难怪没人来报。」

「全都是猜测，麦克艾弗。」布莱恩爵士说，「他们可能只是蛰伏起来，说不定过阵子又在别处冒头了。」

===Original===
“They may be, sir,” answered McIver doggedly. “But take the complete disappearance of Granger a fortnight ago. He’s one of the worst of the Red men, and we know he hasn’t left the country. Where is he? His wife, I happen to know, is crazy with anxiety, so it don’t look like a put-up job. Take that extraordinary case of the Pole who was found lashed to the railings in Whitehall with one half of his beard and hair shaved off and the motto ‘Portrait of a Bolshevist’ painted on his forehead. Well, I don’t need to tell you, sir, that that particular Pole, Strambowski, was undoubtedly a messenger between﻿—well, we know who between and what the message was. And then take last night.”

“Well, what about last night?”

“For the first time this gang has come into direct contact with us.”

“Always assuming the fact of its existence.”

“Exactly, sir,” answered McIver. “Well, they’ve got Zaboleff and they’ve got Waldock, and they laid eight of us out to cool. I guess they’re not to be sneezed at.”

===Chinese===
「也许吧，先生。」麦克艾弗倔强地答道，「可您想想格兰杰（Granger）两礼拜前那一回彻彻底底的失踪。他是最坏的几个赤色分子之一，我们清楚他没出境。他人呢？他太太，我恰好知道，正急得快疯了，所以这不像是什么苦肉计。您再想想那个离奇的案子——那个波兰人被绑在白厅（Whitehall）的栏杆上，胡子头发被剃掉了一半，脑门上还写着『一个布尔什维克的画像』几个字。这个嘛，先生，不用我多说您也明白，那个波兰人，斯特兰博夫斯基（Strambowski），分明就是充当传话人的——至于在谁和谁之间传、传的什么话，我们心里都有数。再想想昨晚。」

「好，昨晚又怎么样？」

「这是这伙人头一回跟我们正面撞上。」

「前提是，它确实存在。」

「一点不错，先生。」麦克艾弗答道，「您看，他们抓走了扎博列夫，抓走了沃尔多克，还把我们八个人摆平在外头晾着。我看，这帮人可不是好惹的。」

===Original===
With a thoughtful look on his face Sir Bryan rose and strolled over to the window. Though not prepared to go quite as far as McIver, there were certainly some peculiar elements in the situation﻿—elements which he, as head of a big public department, could not officially allow for an instant, however much it might amuse him as a private individual.

“We must find Zaboleff and Waldok,” he said curtly, without turning round. “Waldok, at any rate, has friends who will make a noise unless he’s forthcoming. And﻿ ﻿…”

But his further remarks were interrupted by the entrance of his secretary with a note.

“For the Inspector, Sir Bryan,” she said, and McIver after a glance at his chief, opened the envelope. For a while he studied the letter in silence, then with an enigmatic smile he rose and handed it to the man by the window.

“No answer, thank you, Miss Forbes,” he said, and when they were once more alone, he began rubbing his hands together softly﻿—a sure sign of being excited. “Curtis and Samuel Bauer, both flogged nearly to death and found in a slum off Whitechapel. The note said two of ’em had been flogged.”

===Chinese===
布莱恩爵士面带沉思地起身，踱到窗前。虽说他还不像麦克艾弗走得那么远，但这局面里确实有些古怪的成分——这种成分，他身为一个大公共部门的首长，哪怕片刻也不能在公事上容忍，尽管作为一个寻常百姓，他也许看得直乐。

「我们必须找到扎博列夫和沃尔多克。」他头也不回地简短说，「至少沃尔多克是有朋友的，人若交不出来，他们可要闹。还有……」

可他下面的话被进来送条子的秘书打断了。

「给督察的，布莱恩爵士。」她说。麦克艾弗看了上司一眼，拆开信封。他默默地把信看了一会儿，然后带着一个意味深长的笑站起身，把它递给窗边那个人。

「不必回信了，谢谢，福布斯小姐。」他说。屋里重新只剩他俩时，他轻轻地搓起了手——这是他来劲时的铁定征兆。「柯蒂斯（Curtis）和塞缪尔·鲍尔（Samuel Bauer），都几乎被打了个半死，扔在白教堂（Whitechapel）旁边的一片贫民窟里。条子上说，挨了鞭子的是两个家伙。」

===Original===
“So,” said Sir Bryan quietly. “These two were at Barking last night?”

“They were, sir,” answered the Inspector.

“And their line?” queried the Chief.

“White slave traffic of the worst type,” said McIver. “They generally drug the girls with cocaine or some dope first. What do you say to my theory now, sir?”

“It’s another point in its favour, McIver,” conceded Sir Bryan cautiously: “but it still wants a lot more proof. And, anyway, whether you’re right or not, we can’t allow it to continue. We shall be having questions asked in Parliament.”

===Chinese===
「哦。」布莱恩爵士不动声色，「这两位昨晚也在巴金？」

「在的，先生。」督察答道。

「干哪一行的？」上司问。

「最恶劣的那种贩卖白奴的勾当。」麦克艾弗说，「他们一般是先给姑娘们下可卡因或者别的什么药。先生，现在您怎么看我的推断？」

「又多了一个支持它的证据，麦克艾弗。」布莱恩爵士审慎地让步道，「不过要坐实，还差得远。再说，无论你对不对，这种事都不能让它继续下去。再这样下去，议会里都要有人质询了。」

===Original===
McIver nodded portentously. “If I can’t lay my hands on a man who can lift me up like a baby and dope me, may I never have another case. Like a baby, sir. Me﻿—”

He opened his hands out helplessly, and this time Sir Bryan laughed outright, only to turn with a quick frown as the door leading to the secretary’s office was flung open to admit a man. He caught a vague glimpse of the scandalized Miss Forbes hovering like a canary eating birdseed in the background: then he turned to the newcomer.

“Confound it, Hugh,” he cried. “I’m busy.”

===Chinese===
麦克艾弗煞有介事地点点头。「要是我抓不到那个能像提婴孩一样把我提起来下药的家伙，我这辈子就别再办案了。跟婴孩一样，先生。我——」

他无可奈何地摊开双手，这一回布莱恩爵士大笑出声。可他笑声未落，便皱起眉头——通向秘书办公室的那扇门被人一把推开，放进来一个男人。他隐隐瞥见大惊失色的福布斯小姐，像只啄食的金丝雀似的在背景里扑腾：随即转向那位不速之客。

「真见鬼，休（Hugh），」他喊道，「我正忙着呢。」

===Original===
Hugh Drummond grinned all over his face, and lifting a hand like a leg of mutton he smote Sir Bryan in the back, to the outraged amazement of Inspector McIver.

“You priceless old bean,” boomed Hugh affably. “I gathered from the female bird punching the whatnot outside that the great brain was heaving, but, my dear old lad, I have come to report a crime. A crime which I positively saw committed with my own eyes: an outrage: a blot upon this fair land of ours.”

He sank heavily into a chair and selected a cigarette. He was a vast individual with one of those phenomenally ugly faces which is rendered utterly pleasant by the extraordinary charm of its owner’s expression. No human being had ever been known to be angry with Hugh for long. He was either moved to laughter by the perennial twinkle in the big man’s blue eyes, or he was stunned by a playful blow on the chest from a fist which rivalled a steam hammer. Of brain he apparently possessed a minimum: of muscle he possessed about five ordinary men’s share.

===Chinese===
休·德拉蒙德（Hugh Drummond）满脸堆笑，举起一只像羊腿似的大手，朝布莱恩爵士背上擂了一下，看得麦克艾弗督察又骇又怒。

「你这块稀世珍豆啊，」休和和气气地瓮声瓮气，「我从外头那个敲玩意儿的女雀儿那儿打听到，那颗了不起的大脑正在翻江倒海呢。不过，我亲爱的老伙计，我是来报案的。一桩我亲眼所见的罪案：一桩暴行：咱们这片美丽国土上的一个污点。」

他一屁股陷进椅子，挑了一根烟。他是个大块头，长着一张丑得出奇的脸，可脸上那种非同寻常的魅力，却把这张脸变得讨人喜欢得不得了。从没有人能跟休生气生上多久——要么被这大个子蓝眼睛里那点常年的闪光逗得忍俊不禁，要么被他那一拳能跟蒸汽锤媲美的拳头闹着在胸口一捶、捶得发懵。论脑子，他显然少得可怜；论肌肉，他大约抵得上五个寻常汉子。

===Original===
And yet unlike so many powerful men his quickness on his feet was astounding﻿—as many a good heavyweight boxer had found to his cost. In the days of his youth Hugh Drummond﻿—known more familiarly to his intimates as Bulldog﻿—had been able to do the hundred in a shade over ten seconds. And though the mere thought of such a performance now would have caused him to break out into a cold sweat, he was still quite capable of a turn of speed which many a lighter-built man would have envied.

Between him and Sir Bryan Johnstone existed one of those friendships which are founded on totally dissimilar tastes. He had been Bryan Johnstone’s fag at school, and for some inscrutable reason the quiet scholarship of the elder boy had appealed to the kid of fourteen who was even then a mass of brawn. And when one day Johnstone, going about his lawful occasions as a prefect, discovered young Drummond reducing a boy two years older than himself to a fair semblance of a jelly, the appeal was reciprocated.

===Chinese===
然而跟许多大力士不同，他脚下灵活得惊人——好些个有分量的重量级拳手都为此吃过亏。在年轻那会儿，休·德拉蒙德——熟人都更亲热地叫他「斗牛犬（Bulldog）」——百码跑只需十秒出头一点。如今光是想到这种事，就够他冒一身冷汗了，可他真要撒开腿，那份速度仍足以让许多身材更轻盈的人羡慕。

他和布莱恩·约翰斯通爵士之间那份交情，正是建立在两人趣味天差地别之上的那种友谊。当年在学堂里，他是布莱恩·约翰斯通的跟班小厮，出于某种说不清道不明的原因，那位年长男孩身上沉静的学究气，竟对这个十四岁、那时已是一身蛮肉的孩子有了吸引力。等到有一天，约翰斯通以级长之尊照例巡视，撞见小德拉蒙德正把一个比他大两岁的男孩揍得有模有样地瘫成一摊软冻，这种吸引力便成了双向的。

===Original===
“He called you a scut,” said Drummond a little breathlessly when his lord and master mildly inquired the reason of the disturbance. “So I scutted him.”

It was only too true, and with a faint smile Johnstone watched the “scutted” one depart with undignified rapidity. Then he looked at his fag.

“Thank you, Drummond,” he remarked awkwardly.

“Rot. That’s all right,” returned the other, blushing uncomfortably.

And that was all. But it started then, and it never died, though their ways lay many poles apart. To Johnstone a well-deserved knighthood and a high position in the land: to Drummond as much money as he wanted and a life of sport.

===Chinese===
「他骂你是杂种，」当那位少爷兼主子温和地询问这场骚乱因何而起时，德拉蒙德略带气喘地说，「所以我就把他『杂种』了。」

这话一点都不假，约翰斯通带着淡淡的笑，看着那个被『杂种』了的人狼狈地飞快溜走。然后他看向自己的小跟班。

「谢谢你，德拉蒙德。」他别扭地说。

「得了。这不算什么。」对方回答，难受地红了脸。

事情就这么简单。可这份交情从那一刻起，便再没断过，纵使他们走的路南辕北辙。约翰斯通得到了实至名归的爵士封号和举国的高位；德拉蒙德则得到了他想要的那么多钱，和一辈子的游乐打猎。

===Original===
“Has someone stolen the goldfish?” queried Sir Bryan with mild sarcasm.

“Great Scott! I hope not,” cried Hugh in alarm. “Phyllis gave me complete instructions about the brutes before she toddled off. I make a noise like an ant’s egg, and drop them in the sink every morning. No, old lad of the village, it is something of vast import: a stain upon the escutcheon of your force. Last night﻿—let us whisper it in Gath﻿—I dined and further supped not wisely but too well. In fact I deeply regret to admit that I became a trifle blotto﻿—not to say tanked. Of course it wouldn’t have happened if Phyllis had been propping up the jolly old home, don’t you know: but she’s away in the country with the nightingales and slugs and things. Well, as I say, in the young hours of the morning, I thought I’d totter along home. I’d been with some birds﻿—male birds, Tumkins”﻿—he stared sternly at Sir Bryan, while McIver stiffened into rigid horror at such an incredible nickname﻿—“male birds, playing push halfpenny or some such game of skill or chance. And when I left it was about two a.m. Well, I wandered along through Leicester Square, and stopped just outside Scott’s to let one of those watering carts water my head for me. Deuced considerate driver he was too: stopped his horse for a couple of minutes and let one jet play on me uninterruptedly. Well, as I say, while I was lying in the road, steaming at the brow, a motor car went past, and it stopped in Piccadilly Circus.”

===Chinese===
「有人偷了金鱼？」布莱恩爵士带着几分讽刺地问。

「老天！可别！」休吓得叫起来，「菲莉丝（Phyllis）开溜之前，可把那些畜生的注意事项一条一条交代给我了。我每天早上学蚂蚁蛋叫，再把它们丢进水槽里。不不不，我的老乡老伙计，我此来是为了一件了不得的大事：是你们这一行头上的一块污点。昨儿夜里——咱们得悄悄说，只当是在迦特耳语——我吃了饭，又喝了酒，喝得不算明智，反倒过了头。老实说，我很沉痛地承认，我喝得有点儿上头——简直可以说是灌了个饱。当然了，要是菲莉丝在家撑着这个可爱的旧窝，这种事断不会发生，您说是吧：可她跑到乡下，跟夜莺啊蛞蝓啊那些玩意儿作伴去了。话又说回来，我说着，凌晨时分，我琢磨着该晃悠回家了。我跟几个哥们儿——公雀儿，邓金斯（Tumkins）」他板着脸盯着布莱恩爵士，麦克艾弗一听这么个匪夷所思的绰号，惊得直挺挺地僵住——「公雀儿，在一块儿推半便士啦，或者诸如此类的碰运气比技巧的游戏。我离开的时候大概是凌晨两点。之后我晃晃悠悠穿过莱斯特广场（Leicester Square），在斯科特（Scott's）店门口停下，让一辆洒水车给我脑袋降降温。那车夫体贴极了：勒住马停了两分钟，让一道水柱毫不间断地浇在我头上。话说回来，我正躺在路上、脑门上冒着热气的时候，一辆汽车开了过去，在皮卡迪利广场停了下来。」

===Original===
McIver’s air of irritation vanished suddenly, and a quick glance passed between him and Sir Bryan.

“Nothing much you observe in that, Tumkins,” he burbled on, quite unconscious of the sudden attention of his hearers. “But wait, old lad﻿—I haven’t got to the motto yet. From this car there stepped large numbers of men: at least, so it seemed to me, and you must remember I’d recently had a shampoo. And just as I got abreast of them they lifted out another warrior, who appeared to me to be unconscious. At first I thought there were two, until I focused the old optics and found I’d been squinting. They put him on the pavement and got back into the car again just as I tottered alongside.

“ ‘What ho! souls,’ I murmured, ‘what is this and that, so to speak?’

“ ‘Binged, old bean, badly binged,’ said the driver of the car. ‘We’re leaving him there to cool.’

“And with that the car drove off. There was I, Tumkins, in a partially binged condition alone in Piccadilly Circus with a bird in a completely binged condition.

===Chinese===
麦克艾弗那一脸不耐烦的神情一下子没了，他和布莱恩爵士之间飞快地交换了一个眼色。

「这您瞧着没什么稀奇，邓金斯，」他兀自喋喋不休，浑然不觉听他说话的人一下子都来了精神，「但您等等，老伙计——我还没讲到题词呢。从那车上下来了一大帮汉子：至少我看着是这样，您得记得，我那会儿刚被水冲过脑袋。就在我跟他们并排的时候，他们又从车上抬下来一位好汉，我看他是人事不省。起先我以为有两个，等我把这双老眼对准焦距，才发现自己看成了斗鸡眼。他们把那人搁在人行道上，正要回车上，我也就歪歪扭扭地凑了过去。

「『喂！伙计们，』我含糊地嘀咕，『这是演的哪一出啊？』

「『醉死啦，老兄，醉得不成样子。』开车的那个说，『我们把他丢在这儿醒醒酒。』

「说完车就开走了。邓金斯，您想啊，就剩下我一个半醉的人，独自在皮卡迪利广场，守着一个全醉的家伙。

===Original===
“ ‘How now,’ I said to myself. ‘Shall I go and induce yon water merchant to return’﻿—as a matter of fact I was beginning to feel I could do with another whack myself﻿—‘or shall I leave you here﻿—as your pals observed﻿—to cool?’

“I bent over him as I pondered this knotty point, and as I did so, Tumkins, I became aware of a strange smell.”

Hugh paused dramatically and selected another cigarette, while Sir Bryan flashed a quick glance of warning at McIver, who was obviously bursting with suppressed excitement.

“A peculiar and sickly odour, Tumkins,” resumed the speaker with maddening deliberation. “A strange and elusive perfume. For a long time it eluded me﻿—that smell: I just couldn’t place it. And then suddenly I got it: right in the middle, old boy﻿—plumb in the centre of the windpipe. It was chloroform: the bird wasn’t drunk﻿—he was doped.”

===Chinese===
「『怎么着，』我心想，『我该去把那位洒水师傅请回来呢——』——其实我自己也正觉得再来一通也挺好——『还是把你留在这儿——按你那帮哥们儿说的——晾着醒酒？』

「我一边琢磨这个两难的问题，一边俯下身去看他，就在那时候，邓金斯，我闻到了一股怪味儿。」

休煞有介事地顿住，又挑了一根烟，布莱恩爵士则飞快地向明显憋着一肚子兴奋的麦克艾弗投去一个警告的眼色。

「一股淡淡的、有点发腻的怪味，邓金斯。」说话的人用一种叫人抓狂的从容接着说，「一种古怪又飘忽的气息。有很长一段时间我都拿不准——那股味儿：就是辨不出来。后来我突然想通了：正中要害，老伙计——正正卡在气管当中。是氯仿：那家伙不是喝醉了——是被人下了药。」

===Original===
Completely exhausted Hugh lay back in his chair, and once again Sir Bryan flashed a warning glance at his exasperated subordinate.

“Would you be able to recognize any of the men in the car if you saw them again?” he asked quietly.

“I should know the driver,” answered Hugh after profound thought. “And the bird beside him. But not the others.”

“Did you take the number of the car?” snapped McIver.

“My dear old man,” murmured Hugh in a pained voice, “who on earth ever does take the number of a car? Except your warriors, who always get it wrong. Besides, as I tell you, I was partially up the pole.”

“What did you do then?” asked Sir Bryan.

===Chinese===
休像是彻底累垮了，瘫回椅子里。布莱恩爵士又一次向他那位快要按捺不住的下属投去警告的一眼。

「要是再见到，车上那些人里头，你能认出谁来吗？」他不动声色地问。

「我能认出开车的那个。」休深思良久后答道，「还有他旁边那个。别的就认不出了。」

「你记车牌号了吗？」麦克艾弗急问。

「我亲爱的老伙计，」休用一种受了委屈的口吻低声说，「到底有谁会去记车牌号啊？除了您手下的那些勇士，而他们老是记错。再说，我跟您说了，我当时有点儿云里雾里。」

「后来你怎么办的？」布莱恩爵士问。

===Original===
“Well, I brought the brain to bear,” answered Hugh, “and decided there was nothing to do. He was doped, and I was bottled﻿—so by a unanimous casting vote of one﻿—I toddled off home. But Tumkins, while I was feeding the goldfish this morning﻿—or rather after lunch﻿—conscience was gnawing at my vitals. And after profound meditation, and consulting with my fellow Denny, I decided that the call of duty was clear. I came to you, Tumkins, as a child flies to its mother. Who better, I thought, than old Tum-tum to listen to my maidenly secrets? And so﻿ ﻿…”

“One moment, Hugh,” Sir Bryan held up his hand. “Do you mind if I speak to Inspector McIver for a moment?”

“Anything you like, old lad,” murmured Drummond. “But be merciful. Remember my innocent wife in the country.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，我就动用了一下脑瓜子，」休答道，「琢磨着也没什么可做的。他是被下了药，我是被酒灌了——所以经我本人一致通过并投下决定性的一票——我就溜达回家了。不过邓金斯，今天早上我喂金鱼的时候——其实是午饭后——良心可把我折磨坏了。我深思冥想一番，又跟我的伙伴丹尼（Denny）商量了商量，认定天职在召唤。我这才来找您，邓金斯，就像孩子扑向妈妈那样。我想，倾听我这点黄花闺女般的心事，还有谁比老邓邓更合适呢？所以嘛……」

「等一下，休。」布莱恩爵士抬手，「我跟麦克艾弗督察说几句话，介意吗？」

「您请便，老伙计。」德拉蒙德低声说，「不过手下留情。可别忘了我那无辜的老婆还在乡下呢。」

===Original===
And silence settled on the room, broken only by the low-voiced conversation between McIver and his chief in the window. By their gestures it seemed as if Sir Bryan was suggesting something to his subordinate to which that worthy officer was a little loath to agree. And after a while a strangled snore from the chair announced that Drummond was ceasing to take an intelligent interest in things mundane.

“He’s an extraordinary fellow, McIver,” said Sir Bryan, glancing at the sleeper with a smile. “I’ve known him ever since we were boys at school. And he’s not quite such a fool as he makes himself out. You remember that extraordinary case over the man Peterson a year or so ago. Well, it was he who did the whole thing. His complete disability to be cunning utterly defeated that master-crook, who was always looking for subtlety that wasn’t there. And of course his strength is absolutely phenomenal.”

===Chinese===
屋里重归安静，只剩下窗前麦克艾弗和上司低声的交谈。从他们的手势看，像是布莱恩爵士在向下属提议什么，而这位正经的警官不太情愿答应。过了一阵，椅子里传来一声被扼住的鼾声，宣告德拉蒙德已不再对尘世俗务保持清醒的关注。

「他这人可不寻常，麦克艾弗。」布莱恩爵士笑着瞥了那位睡客一眼，「我们从小在学堂里就认识了。他也并不像他自己表现出来的那么蠢。你大概还记得一年多前那个关于彼得森（Peterson）的离奇案子。嘿，整件事就是他一手办的。他那份全然不会耍心眼的本事，彻底击败了那个巨恶——那家伙一直防着根本不存在的圈套。当然啦，他的力气也着实骇人。」

===Original===
“I know, sir,” said McIver doubtfully, “but would he consent to take on such a job﻿—and do exactly as he was told?”

They were both looking out of the window, while in the room behind them the heavy breathing of the sleeper rose and fell monotonously. And when the whole audience is asleep it ceases to be necessary to talk in undertones. Which was why Sir Bryan and the Inspector during the next ten minutes discussed certain matters of import which they would not have discussed through megaphones at the Savoy. They concerned Hugh and other things, and the other things particularly were of interest. And they continued discussing these other things until, with a dreadful noise like a racing motor backfiring, the sleeper sat up in his chair and stretched himself.

“Tumkins,” he cried. “I have committed sacrilege. I have slept in the Holy of Holies. Have you decided on my fate? Am I to be shot at dawn?”

===Chinese===
「这我知道，先生。」麦克艾弗迟疑地说，「可他会愿意接这种活儿吗——还乖乖照吩咐办事？」

两个人都望着窗外，身后屋里，那位睡客沉重的呼吸一起一伏，单调乏味。而一旦全体听众都睡死过去，压低嗓门说话便不再是必要的了。正因如此，接下来十分钟里，布莱恩爵士和督察谈论了一些他们就算在萨沃伊（Savoy）饭店用扩音器也不会谈的要紧事。这些事既关乎休，也关乎别的，而那「别的」尤其有意思。他们就这么继续谈论着那「别的」，直到那位睡客发出一声吓人的动静——活像赛车回火——从椅子里直挺挺坐起来，伸了个懒腰。

「邓金斯！」他嚷道，「我犯了渎圣之罪。我居然在至圣所里睡着了。我的下场您定好了吗？是不是天亮就枪毙？」

===Original===
Sir Bryan left the window and sat down at his desk. For a moment or two he rubbed his chin thoughtfully with his left hand, as if trying to make up his mind: then he lay back in his chair and stared at his erstwhile fag.

“Would you like to do a job of work, old man?”

Hugh started as if he had been stung by a wasp, and Sir Bryan smiled.

“Not real work,” he said reassuringly. “But by mere luck last night you saw something which Inspector McIver would have given a good deal to see. Or to be more accurate, you saw some men whom McIver particularly wants to meet.”

“Those blokes in the car, you mean,” cried Hugh brightly.

“Those blokes in the car,” agreed the other. “Incidentally, I may say there was a good deal more in that little episode than you think: and after consultation with McIver I have decided to tell you a certain amount about it, because you can help us, Hugh. You see, you’re one up on McIver: you have at any rate seen those men and he hasn’t. Moreover, you say you could recognize two of them again.”

===Chinese===
布莱恩爵士离开窗前，在桌边坐下。他用左手若有所思地摩挲了一会儿下巴，像是在下决心：然后靠回椅背，盯着他昔日的小跟班。

「你愿意干点活儿吗，老伙计？」

休像是被黄蜂蜇了一下似的跳起来，布莱恩爵士笑了。

「不是真干活。」他安抚道，「不过你昨晚运气好，撞见了一桩麦克艾弗督察出大价钱也想看见的事。或者更准确地说，你看见了几个麦克艾弗特别想见的人。」

「您是说车上那帮家伙。」休眼睛一亮。

「车上那帮家伙。」对方附和，「顺带说一句，那桩小事背后的名堂可比你想的多得多：跟麦克艾弗商量过后，我决定把其中一部分告诉你，因为你帮得上忙，休。你看，你比麦克艾弗多占一分优势：你好歹见过那些人，他没有。况且，你说你能再认出其中两个来。」

===Original===
“Good heavens! Tumkins,” murmured Hugh aghast, “don’t say you want me to tramp the streets of London looking for them.”

Sir Bryan smiled.

“We’ll spare you that,” he answered. “But I’d like you to pay attention to what I’m going to tell you.”

Hugh’s face assumed the look of intense pain always indicative of thought in its owner.

“Carry on, old bird,” he remarked. “I’ll try and last the course.”

“Last night,” began Sir Bryan quietly, “a very peculiar thing happened to McIver. I won’t worry you with the full details, and it will be enough if I just give you a bare outline of what occurred. He and some of his men in the normal course of duty surrounded a certain house in which were some people we wanted to lay our hands on. To be more accurate there was one man there whom we wanted. He’d been shadowed ever since he’d landed in England that morning, shadowed the whole way from the docks to the house. And sure enough when McIver and his men surrounded the house, there was our friend and all his pals in one of the downstairs rooms. It was then that this peculiar thing happened. I gather from McIver that he heard the noise of an owl hooting, also a faint scuffle and a curse. And after that he heard nothing more. He was chloroformed from behind, and went straight out of the picture.”

===Chinese===
「老天！邓金斯，」休倒抽一口凉气，「您该不是要我去伦敦大街上一个个地找他们吧。」

布莱恩爵士笑了。

「这桩苦差就饶了你吧。」他答道，「不过，接下来我要跟你讲的，你可给我上心听着。」

休的脸作出一副万分痛苦的表情——这在他脸上，向来是动脑子的征兆。

「您讲吧，老伙计。」他说，「我尽量撑到底。」

「昨天夜里，」布莱恩爵士平缓地开了口，「麦克艾弗撞上了一桩很古怪的事。详情我就不烦你了，只把经过大致给你讲一讲就够了。他带着几个手下，按公事的套路，包围了一栋房子，里头有几个人我们正想抓。更准确地说，其中有一个人是我们想要的。这人那天早上刚在英格兰上岸就被人盯上了，一路从码头跟到那栋房子。果不其然，麦克艾弗他们一围住房子，我们那位朋友和他所有的同伙都在楼下的一间屋里。古怪的事就发生在那个时候。我从麦克艾弗那儿得知，他听到了一声猫头鹰叫，还有一阵隐约的扭打声和一句咒骂。之后他就什么都没听见。他从背后被人用氯仿迷翻，直接出局。」

===Original===
“Great Scott!” murmured Hugh, staring incredulously at McIver. “What an amazing thing!”

“And this is where you come in, Hugh,” continued Sir Bryan.

“Me!” Hugh sat up abruptly. “Why me?”

“One of the men inside the room was an interesting fellow known as Flash Jim. He is a burglar of no mean repute, though he is quite ready to tackle any sort of job which carries money with it. And when McIver, having recovered himself this morning, ran Flash Jim to ground in one of his haunts, he was quite under the impression that the men who had doped him and the other officers were pals of Flash Jim. But after he’d talked to him he changed his mind. All Flash Jim could tell him was that on the previous night he and some friends had been discussing business at this house. He didn’t attempt to deny that. He went on to say that suddenly the room had been filled with a number of masked men, and that he’d had a clip over the back of the head which knocked him out. After that presumably he was given a whiff of chloroform to keep him quiet, and the next thing he remembers is being kicked into activity by the policeman at﻿—” Sir Bryan paused a moment to emphasize the point﻿—“at Piccadilly Circus.”

===Chinese===
「老天哪！」休瞪着麦克艾弗，一副不敢相信的样子，「真神了！」

「这就要轮到你登场了，休。」布莱恩爵士接着说。

「我！」休猛地坐直，「干嘛找我？」

「屋里那帮人里头，有一个挺有意思的家伙，人称闪电吉姆。他是个不小的飞贼，不过只要是带钱的活儿，他什么生意都肯接。今早麦克艾弗缓过劲来之后，在闪电吉姆的一个老窝里把他揪了出来，起初他还以为把他和手下迷翻的人是闪电吉姆的同伙。可谈过之后他就改了主意。闪电吉姆能告诉他的只有：头天夜里，他和几个朋友在那栋房子里谈生意。这一点他并不打算否认。他接着说，突然间屋里就挤满了好些蒙面人，他后脑勺上挨了一下就被打晕了。那之后，想必是被人喷了一口氯仿叫他老实待着，他记得的下一件事，就是被某地当班的警察一脚踹醒——」布莱恩爵士顿了顿，加重语气，「——在皮卡迪利广场。」

===Original===
“Good Lord!” said Hugh dazedly. “Then that bird I saw last night sleeping it off on the pavement was Flash Jim.”

“Precisely,” answered Sir Bryan. “But what is far more to the point, old man, is that the two birds you think you would be able to recognize and who were in the car, are two of the masked men who first of all laid out McIver and subsequently surrounded Flash Jim and his pals inside.”

“But what did they want to do that for?” asked Hugh in bewilderment.

“That is just what we want to find out,” replied Sir Bryan. “As far as we can see at the moment they are not criminals in the accepted sense of the word. They flogged two of the men who were there last night, and there are no two men in England who more richly deserved it. They kidnapped two others, one of whom was the man we particularly wanted. Then to wind up, they planted Flash Jim as I’ve told you, let the others go, and brought McIver and all his men back to McIver’s house, where they left them to cool on the pavement.”

===Chinese===
「我的天！」休晕头转向地说，「那么我昨晚看见躺在人行道上醒酒的那家伙，就是闪电吉姆喽。」

「正是。」布莱恩爵士答道，「更要紧的是，老伙计，你说你能认出来的、车上那两位，正是头一批摆平麦克艾弗、随后又在屋里围住闪电吉姆一伙的那帮蒙面人中的两个。」

「可他们干嘛要这么干呢？」休一脸困惑。

「这正是我们要查的。」布莱恩爵士答道，「就眼下看，他们不是通常意义上的罪犯。他们把昨晚在场的两个家伙抽了一顿鞭子，全英国再没有比这两人更该挨鞭子的了。他们还绑架了另外两个，其中一个正是我们特别想要的人。最后收尾的时候，他们按我说的把闪电吉姆丢在那儿，放了其余的人，又把麦克艾弗和他全部手下运回麦克艾弗家，扔在人行道上晾着。」

===Original===
For a moment there was silence, and then Hugh began to shake with laughter.

“But how perfectly priceless!” he spluttered when he was able to speak once more. “Old Algy will burst a blood-vessel when I tell him: you know, Algy, Tumkins, don’t you﻿—that bird with the eyeglass, and the funny-looking face?”

Inspector McIver frowned heavily. All along he had doubted the wisdom of telling Drummond anything: now he felt that his misgivings were confirmed. What on earth was the good of expecting such an obvious ass to be of the smallest assistance? And now this raucous hilarity struck him as being positively indecent. But the Chief had insisted: the responsibility was his. One thing was certain, reflected McIver grimly. Algy, whoever he was, would not be the only one to whom the privilege of bursting a blood-vessel would be accorded. And before very long it would be all round London﻿—probably in the papers. And McIver particularly did not want that to happen. However, the next instant Sir Bryan soothed some of his worst fears.

===Chinese===
一时无人作声，随即休笑得浑身直抖。

「这可太绝了！」他好容易才笑喘着气说出话来，「我告诉老阿尔吉（Algy），他非爆血管不可：您认识阿尔吉的，邓金斯，是不是——那个戴单片眼镜、一脸怪模怪样的家伙？」

麦克艾弗督察重重地皱起眉。他一直就怀疑把这事告诉德拉蒙德是否明智：眼下他觉得自己的顾虑被证实了。指望这么个显而易见的活宝能派上点用场，到底图个什么？而此刻这阵刺耳的狂笑，更让他觉得简直不成体统。可长官坚持如此：责任由长官担。有一件事是肯定的，麦克艾弗闷闷地想，爆血管这份殊荣，无论那阿尔吉是谁，绝不会只落在他一个人头上。用不了多久，这事就会传遍伦敦——多半还会见报。麦克艾弗最不希望的就是这种事。不过下一刻，布莱恩爵士就安抚了他最严重的几重担忧。

===Original===
“Under no circumstances, Hugh,” he remarked gravely, “is Algy to be given a chance of bursting any blood-vessel. You understand what I mean. What I have said to you this afternoon is for you alone﻿—and no one else. We know it: Flash Jim and Co. know it.”

“And the jolly old masked sportsmen know it,” said Hugh.

“Quite,” remarked Sir Bryan. “And that’s a deuced sight too many already. We don’t want any more.”

“As far as I am concerned, my brave Tumkins,” cried the other, “the list is closed. Positively not another participator in the stable secret. But I still don’t see where I leap in and join the fray.”

===Chinese===
「无论如何，休，」他郑重地说，「都不能给阿尔吉任何爆血管的机会。你懂我的意思。今天下午我跟你说的这番话，只许你一个人知道——不许外传。我们知情：闪电吉姆那伙人知情。」

「那些快活的蒙面运动健将也知情。」休说。

「不错。」布莱恩爵士说，「知情人已经太多太多了。我们不希望再多一个。」

「就我这边而言，我勇敢的邓金斯，」对方叫道，「名单就此封口。这马厩里的秘密，绝不再添一个分享者。可我还是没看出来，我打哪儿纵身一跃、加入战团呢？」

===Original===
“This way, old boy,” said Sir Bryan. “McIver is a very strong man, and yet he was picked up last night as he himself says as if he was a baby, by one of these masked men who, judging from a note he wrote, is presumably the leader of the gang. And so we deduce that this leader is something exceptional in the way of strength.”

“By Gad! that’s quick, Tumkins,” said Hugh admiringly. “But then you always did have the devil of a brain.”

“Now you are something very exceptional in that line, Hugh,” continued the other.

“Oh! I can push a fellah’s face if it’s got spots and things,” said Hugh deprecatingly.

“And what I want to know is this. If we give you warning would you care to go with McIver the next time he has any job on, where he thinks it is likely this gang may turn up? We have a pretty shrewd idea as to the type of thing they specialize in.”

===Chinese===
「这么个跃法，老伙计。」布莱恩爵士说，「麦克艾弗是个非常壮实的人，可昨夜他自己说，他像婴孩一样被人提了起来——是那些蒙面人里的一个干的，而从那人留的一张条子看，他多半就是这伙人的头儿。因此我们推断，这位头儿在力气上是个异乎寻常的人物。」

「老天！这推得真快，邓金斯。」休钦佩地说，「不过您向来脑瓜子灵光得很。」

「而在这一项上，你可是非同寻常地厉害，休。」对方接着说。

「哦！要是谁脸上有麻子什么的，我能把他的脸揉一揉。」休谦虚地说。

「我想知道的是这个。要是事先通知你，下一次麦克艾弗出任务、且他认为这伙人可能出现的时候，你愿意不愿意跟他一块儿去？对他们专做哪一类事，我们心里颇有几分数。」

===Original===
Hugh passed his hand dazedly over his forehead.

“Sort of mother’s help you mean,” and McIver frowned horribly. “While the bird biffs McIver, I biff the bird. Is that the notion?”

“That is the notion,” agreed Sir Bryan. “Of course you’ll have to do exactly what McIver tells you, and the whole thing is most unusual. But in view of the special features of the case﻿ ﻿… What is it, Miss Forbes?” He glanced up at his secretary, who was standing in the doorway, with a slight frown.

“He insists on seeing you at once, Sir Bryan.”

She came forward with a card, which Sir Bryan took.

“Charles Latter.” The frown deepened. “What the deuce does he want?”

===Chinese===
休晕乎乎地伸手抹了抹脑门。

「您是说我充当那种妈妈的帮手，」麦克艾弗骇人地皱起眉，「等那家伙放倒麦克艾弗，我再放倒那家伙。是这个意思吧？」

「就是这个意思。」布莱恩爵士附和道，「当然，麦克艾弗叫你做什么你就得做什么，整件事极不寻常。可鉴于本案的特殊之处……什么事，福布斯小姐？」他抬起头，略皱着眉看向站在门口的秘书。

「他非要立刻见您，布莱恩爵士。」

她递上一张名片，布莱恩爵士接了过去。

「查尔斯·拉特（Charles Latter）。」他的眉头皱得更紧了，「他来干什么？」

===Original===
The answer was supplied by the gentleman himself, who appeared at that moment in the doorway. He was evidently in a state of great agitation and Sir Bryan rose.

“I am engaged at the moment, Mr. Latter,” he said coldly.

“My business won’t take you a minute, Sir Bryan,” he cried. “But what I want to know is this. Is this country civilized or is it not? Look at what I received by the afternoon post.”

He handed a sheet of paper to the other, who glanced at it casually. Then suddenly the casual look vanished, and Sir Bryan sat down at his desk, his eyes grim and stern.

“By the afternoon post, you say.”

“Yes. And there have been too many disappearances lately!”

“How did you know that?” snapped the chief, staring at him.

For a moment Latter hesitated and changed colour.

“Oh! everyone knows it,” he answered, trying to speak casually.

“Everyone does not know it,” remarked Sir Bryan quietly. “However, you did quite right to come to me. What are your plans during the next few days?”

“I am going out of London tomorrow to stay with Lady Manton near Sheffield,” answered Latter. “A semipolitical house party. Good heavens! What’s that?”

===Chinese===
答案由那位先生亲自奉上——他此刻已出现在门口。他显然激动万分，布莱恩爵士站起身来。

「我这会儿正有事，拉特先生。」他冷冷地说。

「我的事不占您一分钟，布莱恩爵士。」他叫道，「可我只想知道一件事。这个国家到底还算不算文明国度？您瞧瞧这个，我下午邮班刚收到的。」

他把一张纸递给对方，布莱恩爵士随意瞥了一眼。突然，那随意的神色没了，他在桌前坐下，目光严峻。

「你说，是下午邮班收到的。」

「没错。最近失踪的人也太多啦！」

「这个你怎么知道的？」上司盯着他，厉声问。

拉特迟疑了一下，变了脸色。

「哦！这事人人皆知啊。」他答道，竭力装作漫不经心。

「并不是人人皆知。」布莱恩爵士不动声色地说，「不过，你来找我倒是找对了。这几天你有什么打算？」

「我明天出城，去谢菲尔德（Sheffield）附近曼顿夫人（Lady Manton）那儿住几天。」拉特答道，「一个半政治性的家庭聚会。老天！那是什么？」

===Original===
With a snort Hugh sat up blinking.

“So sorry, old lad,” he burbled. “I snored: know I did. Late hours are the devil, aren’t they?”

He heaved himself out of his chair, and grinned pleasantly at Latter, who frowned disapprovingly.

“I don’t go in for them myself. Well, Sir Bryan.”

“This matter shall be attended to, Mr. Latter. I will see to it. Good afternoon. I will keep this note.”

“And who was that little funny-face?” said Hugh as the door closed behind Mr. Latter.

“Member of Parliament for a north country constituency,” answered Sir Bryan, still staring at the piece of paper in his hand. “Lives above his income. Keenly ambitious. But I thought he was all right.”

===Chinese===
休哼了一声坐起来，眨巴着眼。

「真对不住，老伙计。」他含糊地说，「我打呼噜了：我知道我打了。熬夜伤身啊，是不是？」

他从椅子里撑起身，对拉特和气地笑了笑，拉特却皱着眉头，一脸不以为然。

「我自个儿可不熬夜。那么，布莱恩爵士。」

「这件事会处理的，拉特先生。我亲自过问。再见。这张条子我留下了。」

「那副怪脸的小个子是谁？」拉特先生门一关上，休就问。

「北部一个选区的下院议员。」布莱恩爵士答道，眼睛还盯着手里那张纸，「入不敷出。野心勃勃。可我原以为他是清白的。」

===Original===
The other two stared at him in surprise.

“What do you mean, sir?” asked McIver at length.

“Our unknown friends do not think so, Mac,” answered the chief, handing his subordinate the note left by Latter. “They are beginning to interest me, these gentlemen.”

“You need a rest, Charles Latter,” read McIver slowly. “We have established a home for people like you where several of your friends await you. In a few days you will join them.”

“There are two things which strike one, McIver,” remarked Sir Bryan, thoughtfully lighting a cigarette. “First and most important: that message and the one you found this morning were written on the same typewriter﻿—the letter *s* is distorted in each case. And, secondly, Mr. Charles Latter appears to have inside information concerning the recent activities of our masked friends which it is difficult to see how he came by. Unless”﻿—he paused and stared out of the window with a frown﻿—“unless they are far more conversant with his visiting list than I am.”

===Chinese===
另外两人都惊讶地望着他。

「您这话什么意思，先生？」麦克艾弗终于问道。

「咱们那几位素未谋面的朋友可不这么想，麦老兄。」上司答道，把拉特留下的条子递给下属，「这帮先生，倒渐渐引起我的兴趣了。」

「『查尔斯·拉特，你该歇歇了。』」麦克艾弗慢慢念道，「『我们已经为像你这样的人办了一处收容所，你的好几位朋友都在那里等你。过几天，你也将去与他们作伴。』」

「有两点很扎眼，麦克艾弗。」布莱恩爵士若有所思地点上一根烟，「头一点，也是最要紧的一点：这张条子跟你今早找到的那张，是同一台打字机打的——两张上的字母 *s* 都打了歪。第二，查尔斯·拉特先生似乎掌握着咱们那些蒙面朋友近来活动的内情，而这内情他是怎么知道的，很难看得出来。除非——」他顿住，皱着眉望向窗外——「除非他们比我还清楚他都跟什么人来往。」

===Original===
McIver’s great jaw stuck out as if made of granite.

“It proves my theory, sir,” he grunted, “but if those jokers try that game on with Mr. Latter they won’t catch me a second time.”

A terrific blow on the back made him gasp and splutter.

“There speaks my hero-boy,” cried Hugh. “Together we will outwit the knaves. I will write and cancel a visit: glad of the chance. Old Julia Manton﻿—face like a horse: house at Sheffield: roped me in, Tumkins﻿—positively stunned me with her verbosity. Ghastly house﻿—but reeks of boodle.”

Sir Bryan looked at him surprised.

“Do you mean to say you are going to Lady Manton’s?”

“I was. But not now. I will stick closer than a brother to Mr. McIver.”

“I think not, old man. You go. If you’d been awake you’d have heard Latter say that he was going there too. You can be of use sooner than I thought.”

“Latter going to old Julia?” Hugh stared at him amazed. “My dear old Tum-tum, what a perfectly amazing coincidence.”

===Chinese===
麦克艾弗那方正的大下巴像花岗岩似地撅了出来。

「这就证实了我的推断，先生。」他咕哝道，「可要是那帮家伙想在拉特先生身上故技重施，他们休想再得我一次手。」

背上重重挨了一下，呛得他直喘气。
